## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Introduction]{.HTitleText1} {#h-795622 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out the
scheme and application of these Regulations and exempts some explosives
from provisions of the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17). It also defines
certain terms that are used in the Regulations, including "explosives".

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 1](#1354344-1399367){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354344-1399367.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 1](#1451337-1452270){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451337-1452270.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-1-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 1"){.PITLink}

s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-2-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 2"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/2022-121,

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scheme of Regulations

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} These
  Regulations are divided into 20 Parts. Some Parts apply to all
  explosives and some apply only to specific types of explosive. The
  final Part applies to restricted components.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirements, prohibitions and
  safety precautions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Part 2 sets out the general requirements,
  prohibitions and safety precautions that apply to every person who is
  carrying out an activity involving an explosive or is in the vicinity
  of an explosive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization and classification of
  explosives

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Part 3 indicates how to obtain an authorization of an
  explosive, how an authorized explosive is classified, how to obtain
  permission to change an authorized explosive, when an authorization
  may be cancelled and when an authorized explosive must be recalled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importing and exporting explosives and
  transporting explosives in transit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Part 4 indicates how to obtain a permit to import or
  export explosives or to transport them in transit and sets out the
  requirements for carrying out those activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturing explosives

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Part 5 indicates how to obtain a factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate and sets out the circumstances in which
  explosives may be manufactured without a licence or certificate. It
  also sets out the requirements for manufacturing explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Magazine licences and storage in
  licensed magazine

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Part 6 indicates how to obtain a magazine licence and
  sets out the requirements for storing explosives in a licensed
  magazine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions of general application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Part 7 sets out the terms and conditions that apply
  to all holders of licences, permits and certificates, indicates how to
  obtain an amendment or renewal and sets out the circumstances in which
  a licence, permit or certificate may be suspended or cancelled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Screening

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Part 8 sets out the screening and supervision
  requirements that apply to people who have, or could have, access to
  high risk explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transporting explosives

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Part 9 sets out the requirements for transporting
  explosives, including transporting them in transit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Particular types of explosives

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Parts 10 to 15 set out the requirements for the
  acquisition, storage and sale of the following types of explosives:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} military explosives and law enforcement explosives
    -- Part 10;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} industrial explosives -- Part 11;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} blank cartridges for tools -- Part 12;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} special purpose explosives -- Part 13;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} small arms cartridges, propellant powder and
    percussion caps -- Part 14; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} model and high power rocket motors -- Part 15.

  Part 14 also authorizes, and sets out requirements for, the
  manufacture of small arms cartridges and black powder cartouches.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of pyrotechnics

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Parts 16 to 18 set out the requirements for the
  acquisition, storage, sale and use of the following types of
  fireworks:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} consumer fireworks -- Part 16;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} special effect pyrotechnics -- Part 17; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} display fireworks, including firecrackers -- Part
    18.

  Parts 17 and 18 also indicate how to obtain a fireworks operator
  certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Part 19 sets out the fees to be paid for obtaining
  an authorization, permit, licence or certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restricted components

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} Part 20 restricts the sale and acquisition of
  certain components of explosives and sets out requirements for their
  sale and storage.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 37
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399918){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399918.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20160415.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Regulations

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} These
  Regulations apply to all explosives except the following, to which
  only Part 5 applies:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} safety and strike-anywhere matches;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} life-saving devices (for example, signals, flares
    and parachute release devices) that are being carried in an
    aircraft, train, vessel or vehicle as equipment that is necessary
    for its safe operation or for the safety of its occupants;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} explosives classified as UN 3268 by the competent
    authority of their country of origin under the Model Regulations on
    the Transport of Dangerous Goods, published by the United Nations;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} explosives diluted to less than 1% by weight,
    including diluted explosives used as reagents (for example,
    1H-tetrazole), training kits for sniffer dogs and kits to test the
    functioning of machines that detect trace levels of explosives; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} Christmas crackers containing less than 2 mg of
    explosive substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 6(b) to (d) and subsections 9(2) and (3)
  of the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17) do not apply to the explosives
  set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from Act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 6(e) and section 20 of the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17) do not apply to the explosives set out in
  paragraphs (1)(a), (b), (d) and (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from Act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 21 of the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17)
  applies to the explosives set out in subsection (1) only in respect of
  the activities referred to in paragraph 6(a) of that Act, and to the
  explosives set out in paragraph 1(c) only in respect of the activities
  referred to in paragraph 6(e) of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Explosives under control of allied
  armed forces

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Explosives that are under the control of any armed
  forces that are cooperating with the Canadian Forces are deemed to be
  under the direction or control of the Minister of National Defence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 1
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399922){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399922.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the
  purposes of section 2 of the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and
  these Regulations, [[explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an explosive substance or explosive article that is
    not manufactured or used to produce an explosion, detonation or
    pyrotechnic effect but is included in Class 1 of Schedule 1 to the
    [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any substance numbered UN 1442, AMMONIUM
    PERCHLORATE as set out in columns 1 and 2 of Schedule 1 to the
    [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286);

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} any substance numbered UN 3375, AMMONIUM NITRATE
    EMULSION, GEL, OR SUSPENSION, intermediate for blasting explosives,
    as set out in columns 1 and 2 of Schedule 1 to the [Transportation
    of Dangerous Goods Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a multi-ingredient kit that is used to manufacture
    an explosive.

- Definition of [[military device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 2 of the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17), [[military device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
  shell, bomb, projectile, mine, missile, rocket, shaped charge,
  grenade, perforator and any other explosive article that is
  manufactured exclusively for military or law enforcement purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in these Regulations.

  [[activity involving an explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[activity involving an explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      acquiring, possessing, selling, offering for sale, storing,
      manufacturing, transporting, transporting in transit, importing,
      exporting or delivering an explosive or using
      fireworks. ([activité visant un explosif]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[attended]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[attended]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to be constantly monitored
      by a person and, unless these Regulations provide otherwise,
      includes monitoring by a person using electronic
      means. ([surveillé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[compatible]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[compatible]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a
      material, that the material

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} will not react chemically with an explosive or
        raw material so as to affect the functioning of the explosive or
        raw material or to increase the likelihood of an ignition of the
        explosive or raw material; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} will not be degraded by an explosive or raw
        material so as to affect its function or increase the likelihood
        of an ignition of the explosive or raw
        material. ([compatible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[decontaminate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[decontaminate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to completely remove,
      clean or purge an explosive substance from a building, room, area,
      vehicle, equipment or
      container. ([décontamination]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[explosive article]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[explosive article]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an article that
      contains one or more explosive substances. ([objet
      explosif]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[explosive substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[explosive substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a solid or
      liquid substance --- or a mixture of solid and liquid substances
      --- that is capable, by chemical reaction, of producing a gas at a
      temperature, pressure and speed that is capable of causing damage
      to surrounding structures or infrastructure. It includes a
      substance --- or a mixture of substances --- that is designed to
      produce an effect of heat, light, sound, gas or smoke, or a
      combination of such effects, by means of a non-detonative
      self-sustaining exothermic chemical reaction, even if the
      substance or mixture does not produce a gas. ([matière
      explosive]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[industrial explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[industrial explosive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
      as in section 213. ([explosif industriel]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[local authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[local authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to
      special effect pyrotechnics or display fireworks, the municipal,
      provincial or territorial organization or office that has
      authority to authorize their use in a locality. ([autorité
      locale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[manufacturing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[manufacturing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 53. ([fabriquer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[misfire]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[misfire]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the complete or partial
      failure of a charge to explode as
      planned. ([raté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[product name]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[product name]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the trade name or part
      number of an explosive, or both in the case of an explosive that
      has both a trade name and a part number. ([nom de
      produit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[pyrotechnic event]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[pyrotechnic event]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in section 361. ([activité pyrotechnique]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[small arms cartridge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[small arms cartridge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
      as in subsection 268(1). ([cartouches pour armes de petit
      calibre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[special effect pyrotechnics]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[special effect pyrotechnics]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in section 361. ([pièces pyrotechnique à effets
      spéciaux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[storage unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[storage unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a building, structure,
      place or container in which explosives are stored and that is not
      licensed. However, it does not include a dwelling or any
      structure, place or container in a dwelling. ([unité de
      stockage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[vendor magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[vendor magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in section 144. ([licence de poudrière
      (vendeur)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[vulnerable place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[vulnerable place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any building in which people live, work or
        assemble;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} public roads, railways and other transportation
        infrastructure;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} pipelines and energy transmission lines; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any place where a substance that increases the
        likelihood of a fire or explosion is likely to be stored. ([lieu
        vulnérable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The definition [[manufacturing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  in subsection (3) does not apply in Part 20.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspectors' duties

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in these
Regulations has the effect of preventing an inspector from carrying out
their duties under the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic notice

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** Any document, other than a
document referred to in subsection 173(3) or (4) or 183(3) or section
426, and any information that is required by these Regulations to be in
writing, may be delivered in hard copy or by electronic means.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 3](#1451344-1452279){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[General Requirements, Prohibitions and Safety Precautions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-795727 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out
requirements, prohibitions and safety precautions that apply to every
person who is carrying out an activity involving an explosive or who is
in the vicinity of explosives.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399935){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399935.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Requirements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795730 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Age restriction

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise indicated in these Regulations, a person must be at least 18
  years old to carry out an activity involving an explosive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to the following
  persons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who acquires small arms cartridges for
    their own personal use; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person under 18 years old who possesses small
    arms cartridges or black powder cartouches at a federal, provincial
    or territorial site that holds a division 2 factory licence for the
    purposes of conducting demonstrations or historical re-enactments
    sanctioned by the federal, provincial or territorial authority for
    the site.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399939){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399939.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 4](#1451346-1452287){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451346-1452287.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement that explosives be authorized

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** A person may carry out an
activity involving an explosive only if the explosive is authorized
under Part 3.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399943){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399943.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Prohibitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795735 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited explosives

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** The following explosives
are, in the opinion of the Minister, intrinsically unsafe and must not
be acquired, possessed, used or sold:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} trick fireworks (for example, cigarette loads,
  dancing crackers and exploding golf balls); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} explosives containing chemicals that are not
  compatible with one another.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 38
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399947){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399947.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20160415.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale or transfer

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** A person must not sell or
otherwise transfer an explosive to another person if they have
reasonable grounds to suspect that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the other person is not authorized by the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17) or these Regulations to acquire the explosive;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the explosive will be used for a criminal purpose; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the other person is under the influence of alcohol or
  another performance-diminishing substance.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399951){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399951.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition of restricted explosive

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** A person must not acquire
an explosive that the Chief Inspector of Explosives has authorized with
restrictions unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the authorization is restricted to specified
  people or bodies or classes of people or bodies, they are a person or
  body or member of a class of people or bodies specified in the
  authorization;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the authorization is restricted to specified
  purposes, they intend to use the explosive for a purpose specified in
  the authorization; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the authorization is restricted to specified
  people or bodies or classes of people or bodies and to specified
  purposes, they are a person or body or member of a class of people or
  bodies specified in the authorization and intend to use the explosive
  for a purpose specified in the authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399955){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399955.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** A person must not carry
out an activity involving an explosive if they are under the influence
of alcohol or another performance-diminishing substance. A person who
has taken a prescription drug may carry out such an activity if they
have medical proof that they need the drug and that it will not impede
their ability to safely carry out the activity.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399959){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399959.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Smoking

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** A person must not smoke
while they are carrying out an activity involving an explosive or if
they are within 8 m of an explosive.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399963){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399963.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alteration of markings

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not alter, deface or obscure any printing or label on an
  explosive or its packaging unless they are ordered to do so by an
  inspector to correct an error.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of quantity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, after removing an explosive from its
  packaging, a person may alter the printing or label on the packaging
  to reflect the remaining quantity.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399967){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399967.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** A person must not include
in a document required by these Regulations any information that is
false or misleading. A person must not submit a document that, by reason
of non-disclosure of facts, is false or misleading.

### [Safety Precautions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795763 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Knowledge of activity

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** A person who is carrying
out an activity involving an explosive must ensure that they, and any
person under their supervision, have knowledge of the activity being
carried out and of the measures that must be taken to minimize any
likelihood of harm to people and property that could result from the
activity, including measures to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevent an accidental ignition;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} limit the spread of any fire or the extent of any
  explosion; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect people from the effects of any fire or
  explosion.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399971){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399971.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Precautionary measures

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** A person who is carrying
out an activity involving an explosive must take measures that minimize
the likelihood of harm to people or property that could result from the
activity, including measures to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevent an accidental ignition;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} limit the spread of any fire or the extent of any
  explosion; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect people from the effects of any fire or
  explosion.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400164){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400164.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-20-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limiting access to explosives

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** A person who is in
control of an explosive must ensure that only people authorized by them
or by law have access to the explosive.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399978){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399978.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of fireworks

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** A person may use
fireworks only for the purpose for which they were designed.

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Authorization and Classification of Explosives]{.HTitleText1} {#h-795778 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out
activities involving an explosive that may be carried out even if the
explosive is not authorized. It also sets out the procedure for
obtaining authorization of an explosive and when permission is required
to change an explosive that has been authorized. It deals as well with
the classification and reclassification of explosives, their recall and
the cancellation of an authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399982){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399982.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Inspector's delegate

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** The duties and functions
of the Chief Inspector of Explosives that are set out in sections 32 to
40 may be performed by an inspector designated by the Chief Inspector.

### [Authorization Not Required]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795783 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from authorization

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 11, the
following activities involving an explosive may be carried out even
though the explosives are not authorized:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of up to 1 kg of explosives to be
  used in conducting an experiment, demonstration, test or analysis at a
  school, college, university or other learning institution;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of up to 5 kg of explosives to be
  used in conducting an experiment, demonstration, test or analysis by a
  government or law enforcement agency;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of up to 5 kg of explosives to be
  used in conducting an experiment, test or analysis at a private or
  commercial laboratory;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of black powder charges for
  ceremonial use;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of small arms cartridges or black
  powder cartouches for personal use;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the assembly and use of special purpose pyrotechnics,
  as defined in section 361;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the sending of a sample of an explosive to the Chief
  Inspector of Explosives, at his or her request, for authorization
  testing;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the importation of an explosive, if the conditions
  set out in section 45 or 45.1 are met;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the exportation of an explosive, if the conditions
  set out in section 45 are met;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the transportation in transit of an explosive;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the transportation of set pieces, that is to say,
  lattices intended to be fixed to the ground and arrayed with
  ground-level fireworks that form an image, word or design; and

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the transportation of explosives within Canada for
  the Department of National Defence, the Canadian Armed Forces or any
  armed forces cooperating with the Canadian Armed Forces, if the
  following conditions are met:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the explosives have been classified for the
    purposes of transportation by a competent authority under the
    Recommendations on the Transport of Dangerous Goods --- Model
    Regulations, published by the United Nations, as amended from time
    to time, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the competent authority has been recognized by the
    Chief Inspector of Explosives as having a transport classification
    process equivalent to that set out in the [Transportation of
    Dangerous Goods Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399987){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399987.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 5](#1451348-1452295){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451348-1452295.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 25"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application for Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795796 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of authorization

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  explosive may be authorized for an indefinite period or for a
  specified period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indefinite period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An authorization for an indefinite period is issued
  if the explosive is intended to be used in ongoing or recurring
  activities. The authorization may be issued with restrictions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specified period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An authorization for a specified period is issued if
  the explosive is intended to be used for a specific purpose within a
  specified period (for example, a chemical analysis, a product trial,
  scientific research or a special event, tour or international
  competition involving fireworks).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399990){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399990.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicant

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** The following people may
apply to have an explosive authorized:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who proposes to manufacture the explosive;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a foreign manufacturer of the explosive; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who has permission to apply from a
  manufacturer of the explosive.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399994){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399994.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-27-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for indefinite period

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
authorization for an indefinite period must complete, sign and send to
the Chief Inspector of Explosives the application form provided by the
Department of Natural Resources. The application must include the
following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email address
  of the applicant as well as of the manufacturer if the applicant is
  not the manufacturer;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a short description of the explosive and its
  properties, as well as its product name;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the circumstances in which it will
  be used;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for an explosive article, a technical drawing of the
  article, prepared to scale, that sets out its dimensions, its
  components and the materials of its construction;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the composition of the explosive and the percent
  tolerance or range of each of its ingredients;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the composition of any substitute explosive and the
  percent tolerance or range of each of its ingredients;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the results of any tests conducted by or on behalf of
  a foreign state that has authorized the explosive or a similar
  explosive, or the classification of the explosive by a foreign state;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated classification of the explosive under
  section 36;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an explosive to be manufactured in
  Canada for the first time, a description of the manufacturing
  operations that will be used;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} for an explosive article, a description of its
  performance characteristics, the way in which it functions and the
  instructions for its use;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} a description of any packaging or container in which
  the explosive will be handled, used, stored or displayed for sale;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} a description of the packaging or container in which
  the explosive will be transported and stored, and the safety standards
  to which the packaging or container must conform under the
  [Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act, 1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01);

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} the information that will be printed on the explosive
  and its packaging;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} the safety instructions, in both English and French,
  that will accompany the explosive, including procedures for preventing
  accidents when handling, storing, using or destroying the explosive
  and the procedures to follow if the explosive is lost or stolen; and

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} the period after the manufacture of the explosive
  during which it will remain suitable for use in the circumstances
  described in paragraph (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 2
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1399999){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1399999.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 6](#1451353-1452297){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451353-1452297.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for specified period

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
authorization for a specified period, if the explosive is for use other
than at a special event, tour or international competition involving
fireworks, must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of
Explosives the application form provided by the Department of Natural
Resources. The application must include the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email address
  of the applicant as well as of the manufacturer if the applicant is
  not the manufacturer;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the authorization is requested;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a short description of the explosive and its
  properties, as well as its product name;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the circumstances in which it will
  be used;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of the explosive that will be used
  during the period;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} every location where it will be used;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a product trial, the location where
  the explosive will be manufactured;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} for an explosive article, a technical drawing of the
  article prepared to scale that includes its dimensions, its components
  and the materials of its construction;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the composition of the explosive and the percent
  tolerance or range of each of its ingredients;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the composition of any substitute explosive and the
  percent tolerance or range of each of its ingredients;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} for an explosive article, a description of its
  performance characteristics, the way in which it functions and
  instructions for its use;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} a description of any packaging or container in which
  the explosive will be handled, used or displayed for sale;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} a description of the packaging or container in which
  the explosive will be transported and stored and the safety standards
  with which the packaging or container must comply under the
  [Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act, 1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01);

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} the safety instructions, in both English and French,
  that will accompany the explosive, including procedures for preventing
  accidents when handling, storing, using or destroying the explosive
  and the procedures to follow if the explosive is lost or stolen;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} the delivery system, if the explosive is to be
  transported in bulk;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} the method to be used to destroy any of the explosive
  that is not used before the authorization expires; and

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} the results of any tests conducted by or on behalf of
  a foreign state that has authorized the explosive or a similar
  explosive, or the classification of the explosive by a foreign state.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 3
- SOR/2018-231, s. 3
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400004){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400004.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 7](#1451355-1452302){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451355-1452302.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for specified period ---
other activities

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
authorization for a specified period, if the explosive is for use at a
special event, tour or international competition involving fireworks,
must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of Explosives the
application form provided by the Department of Natural Resources. The
application must include the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email address
  of the applicant as well as of the manufacturer if the applicant is
  not the manufacturer;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a short description of the explosive and its
  properties, as well as its product name;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the transport classification issued by the country of
  origin;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the places and dates of the special event, tour or
  international competition at which the explosive will be used;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the controls that will be put in place to ensure that
  the explosive is used only for the special event, tour or
  international competition for which it is authorized;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the precautions that will be taken to minimize the
  likelihood of harm to people and property; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the method to be used to destroy any of the explosive
  that is not used before the authorization expires.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 4, 39
- SOR/2018-231, ss. 4, 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400008){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400008.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 8](#1451360-1452304){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451360-1452304.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** The applicant for an
authorization must pay the applicable fees set out in Part 19.

### [Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795863 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization for indefinite period

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Inspector of Explosives must authorize the use of an explosive
  for an indefinite period if the Chief Inspector determines, on the
  basis of the results of one or more of the following tests, that the
  explosive can be safely manufactured, handled, stored, transported,
  used and destroyed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a test listed in the table to this Part that is
    conducted on the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a test listed in the table to this Part that is
    conducted on a similar explosive; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a test that was conducted on this or a similar
    explosive by or on behalf of a foreign state that has authorized the
    explosive or similar explosive and that is equivalent to a test
    listed in the table to this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization with restrictions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorization must be given with restrictions if
  the Chief Inspector determines, based on the type, hazard
  classification, UN number and the circumstances in which the explosive
  will be used, that it can safely be used only by a specific person,
  body or class of people or bodies, or for a specific purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400012){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400012.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization for specified period

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Inspector of
Explosives must authorize an explosive for a specified period if the
Chief Inspector determines, on the basis of the information in the
application and the results of one or more of the following tests, that
the explosive can be safely manufactured, handled, stored, transported,
used and destroyed:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any sample testing; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a test that was conducted on the explosive or a
  similar explosive by or on behalf of a foreign state that has
  authorized the explosive or similar explosive and that the Chief
  Inspector of Explosives has determined is the equivalent to a test
  listed in the table to this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400016){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400016.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 9](#1451362-1452309){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451362-1452309.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sample required

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  sample of an explosive is required for a test listed in the table to
  this Part, the Chief Inspector of Explosives must notify the
  applicant, specify the quantity of the explosive required and indicate
  the address to which the sample may be sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sending sample

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not send a sample of an explosive for
  testing unless they have been requested to do so by the Chief
  Inspector.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400020){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400020.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Inspector of Explosives must give the applicant written notice
  of whether the explosive has been authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the explosive has not been authorized, the notice
  must include the reasons for the refusal to authorize.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification and restrictions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the explosive has been authorized, the notice must
  include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the classification of the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an authorization for an indefinite
    period, any restrictions respecting the people or bodies or classes
    of people or bodies that may use the explosive and any restrictions
    respecting the purposes for which it may be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an authorization for a specified
    period, the period for which the explosive is authorized, the
    quantity authorized, the place of manufacture and the place of use;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the explosive was authorized.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400025){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400025.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-35-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 35"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Classification of Explosives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795890 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification of authorized explosives

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Inspector of Explosives must classify each authorized explosive
  by type, hazard category and UN number in accordance with this
  section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Type

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each authorized explosive is classified according to
  its intended use as one of the following types:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} E --- high explosives:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} E.1 --- blasting explosives,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} E.2 --- perforating explosives,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} E.3 --- special-application explosives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} I --- initiation systems;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} P --- propellant powder:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} P.1 --- black powder and hazard category PE 1
      black powder substitutes,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} P.2 --- smokeless powder and hazard category PE
      3 black powder substitutes;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} C --- cartridges:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} C.1 --- small arms cartridges,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} C.2 --- blank cartridges for tools,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} C.3 --- percussion caps;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} D --- military explosives and law enforcement
    explosives;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} F --- fireworks:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} F.1 --- consumer fireworks,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} F.2 --- display fireworks,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} F.3 --- special effect pyrotechnics,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} F.4 --- fireworks accessories,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} F.5 --- novelty devices;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} R --- rocket motors:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} R.1 --- model rocket motors,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} R.2 --- high-power rocket motors,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} R.3 --- rocket motor accessories; or

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} S --- special purpose explosives:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} S.1 --- low-hazard special purpose explosives,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} S.2 --- high-hazard special purpose explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hazard category

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each authorized explosive is also classified for the
  purposes of manufacturing and storage into one or more of the
  following potential effects (PE) categories, if applicable. The
  classification is made according to hazard, determined on the basis of
  manufacturing operations, the quantity of explosive and how the
  explosive will be packaged:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} PE 1 --- mass explosion hazard;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} PE 2 --- serious projection hazard but not a mass
    explosion hazard;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} PE 3 --- fire hazard and either a minor blast or
    minor projection hazard, or both, but not a mass explosion hazard;
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} PE 4 --- fire hazard or slight explosion hazard, or
    both, with only local effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}UN number

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each authorized explosive may be assigned a UN number
  as set out in column 1 of Schedule 1 to the [Transportation of
  Dangerous Goods Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286) on the
  basis of its type, hazard category and the circumstances in which it
  will be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 37
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400030){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400030.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 10](#1451364-1452313){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::

:::

### [Authorized Explosives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795931 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Changes to authorized explosive

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has obtained the authorization of an explosive must obtain
  the written permission of the Chief Inspector of Explosives before
  changing the explosive in a way that would render any of the following
  information inaccurate:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an authorization for an indefinite
    period, any information required under paragraphs 28(d) to (f)
    or (l) to (n); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an authorization for a specified
    period, any information required under paragraphs 29(h) to (j), (m)
    or (n).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission given

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Inspector of Explosives must give
  permission if the proposed change would not affect the performance or
  classification of the explosive. The Chief Inspector must notify the
  holder in writing when permission is given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission refused

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If permission is refused, the Chief Inspector must
  notify the holder in writing that permission is refused and that a new
  application for authorization is required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to type C.2, C.3 or S.1
  explosives if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of transportation, the explosive
    has been classified as Class 1.4S under the Model Regulations on the
    Transport of Dangerous Goods, published by the United Nations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the UN number assigned to the explosives by the
    competent authority of the country of origin has not changed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Inspector of Explosives has received a
    written notice of any change to the explosive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 5
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400033){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400033.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reclassification

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Inspector of Explosives must reclassify an authorized explosive
  if periodic testing or new information reveals that its classification
  is no longer appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Inspector must give the person who obtained
  the authorization written notice of the explosive's new
  classification.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400037){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400037.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-38-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 38"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of authorization

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Inspector of
Explosives must cancel the authorization of an explosive in any of the
following circumstances:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who obtained the authorization has not
  paid the applicable fee within 30 days after the date of an invoice
  from the Department of Natural Resources;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} periodic testing or new information reveals that the
  explosive can no longer be safely manufactured, handled, stored,
  transported, used or destroyed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Inspector is unable to determine whether
  the explosive can still be safely manufactured, handled, stored,
  transported, used or destroyed;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person who obtained the authorization requests
  the cancellation; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manufacturer is no longer in business and the
  Chief Inspector has reasonable grounds to believe that the explosive
  is no longer in any person's possession.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400042){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400042.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-39-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 39"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recall

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  authorization of an explosive is cancelled because the explosive is no
  longer safe when manufactured, handled, stored, transported, used or
  destroyed in the normal way, the Chief Inspector of Explosives must,
  by written notice, require any manufacturer, any importer and any
  seller of the explosive to recall any of the explosive that they have
  manufactured, imported or sold.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bad batch or lot

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a batch or lot of explosives cannot be safely
  handled, stored, transported, used or destroyed because of a
  manufacturing defect, the Chief Inspector of Explosives must, by
  written notice, require the manufacturer or importer and any seller of
  the batch or lot to recall it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties upon recall

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who receives a notice to recall an explosive
  must immediately recall the explosive and either make it safe or
  destroy it in a safe manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 6
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400045){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400045.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-40-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 40"){.PITLink}
:::

### [List of Authorized Explosives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-795969 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of list

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must keep an up-to-date list of all explosives that are
  authorized for an indefinite period. The list must set out the
  following information for each explosive:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who obtained the
    authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the product name and classification of the
    explosive; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any restrictions imposed by the Chief Inspector of
    Explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the Minister is not required to include on
  the list an explosive that is classified as a military explosive or
  law enforcement explosive.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 38, 39
- SOR/2018-231, s. 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400049){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400049.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 41"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal from list

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must remove
from the list of authorized explosives any explosive for which the
authorization is cancelled.

::: {}
## [TABLE]{.scheduleLabel}[Tests for Authorizing Explosives]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-795985 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- [1]{.lawlabel} Tests for physical properties --- including
  consistency, reaction rate, rate of moisture-absorption, tendency for
  separation, exudation, behaviour at both high and low temperatures,
  density and specific gravity

- [2]{.lawlabel} Tests for chemical composition --- including the
  determination of the percentage of each ingredient in the explosive

- [3]{.lawlabel} Tests for stability --- including the determination of
  the stability of the explosive by subjecting it to varying
  environmental conditions, such as high temperatures, that might
  produce spontaneous ignition of the explosive or a variation of its
  sensitivity

- [4]{.lawlabel} Tests for ignition behaviour

- [5]{.lawlabel} Tests to determine the potential for mass explosion in
  a fire

- [6]{.lawlabel} Tests to determine whether ignition of an explosive
  article might ignite other explosive articles when stored or
  transported together

- [7]{.lawlabel} Tests for mechanical sensitivity --- including
  sensitivity to friction and impact

- [8]{.lawlabel} Tests for sensitivity to electrostatic discharge

- [9]{.lawlabel} Tests for sympathetic initiation and detonation

- [10]{.lawlabel} Tests for velocity of detonation

- [11]{.lawlabel} Tests for explosive strength

- [12]{.lawlabel} Tests for or calculation of the composition of gases
  that evolve on explosion

- [13]{.lawlabel} Performance tests

- [14]{.lawlabel} Tests for minimum burning pressure

- [15]{.lawlabel} Packaging tests

- [16]{.lawlabel} Any other tests that are necessary for the purpose of
  authorizing an explosive
::::
:::::

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Importing and Exporting Explosives and Transporting Explosives in Transit]{.HTitleText1} {#h-796002 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which explosives may be
  imported, exported or transported in transit without a permit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information that a person must include in an
  application for a permit to import or export explosives or transport
  them in transit; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the requirements that permit holders must meet,
  including the information that permit holders must provide to the
  Chief Inspector of Explosives after explosives are imported, exported
  or transported in transit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400055){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400055.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-43-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 43"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Part.

  [[annual permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[annual permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a permit for multiple
      importations, exportations or transportations in transit during a
      one-year period. ([permis annuel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[secure storage site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[secure storage site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a site at which
      the Minister or a province has authorized storage of the type and
      quantity of explosive that is to be transported in transit. ([lieu
      de stockage sûr]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[single use permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[single use permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a permit for a
      single importation, exportation or transportation in
      transit. ([permis à utilisation unique]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Explosive quantity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A reference to the mass of an explosive in this Part
  is a reference to its net quantity (the mass of the explosive
  excluding the mass of any packaging or container and, in the case of
  an explosive article, also excluding any component that is not an
  explosive substance).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2013-211, s. 500
- SOR/2016-75, s. 38(E)
- SOR/2018-231, s. 7(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400059){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400059.html"
  role="button"}

### [No Permit Required]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796018 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import, export or transport in transit

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** A person may import,
export or transport in transit an explosive set out in the table to this
section without a permit if the following conditions are met:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosive is imported, exported or transported in
  transit for personal use and not for commercial purposes;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the explosive enters or leaves Canada with the person
  importing or exporting it or, if the explosive is transported in
  transit, it remains with the person transporting it at all times; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of small arms cartridges, the cartridges
  do not include a tracer, incendiary or similar military component or
  device; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of the explosive being imported,
  exported or transported in transit is not more than the quantity set
  out in the table.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importation

**[[45.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45.1 .sectionLabel}** A person may import
explosives without a permit if the following conditions are met:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosives have been procured by the Department
  of National Defence, the Canadian Armed Forces or any armed forces
  cooperating with the Canadian Armed Forces;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the shipper and the carrier have ensured that nothing
  other than the explosives are transported in the same vehicle;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the carrier or driver is in possession of an approval
  letter or equivalent document issued under Part 8;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Department of National Defence, the Canadian
  Armed Forces or any armed forces cooperating with the Canadian Armed
  Forces has informed the carrier of the transportation requirements
  under Part 9; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the carrier transports the explosives directly from
  the Canadian port of entry to the Department of National Defence, the
  Canadian Armed Forces or any armed forces cooperating with the
  Canadian Armed Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 12](#1451371-1452326){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451371-1452326.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Import Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796033 .Subheading}

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796034 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant for an import permit must complete, sign and send to the
  Chief Inspector of Explosives the application form provided by the
  Department of Natural Resources. The application must state whether a
  single use permit or an annual permit is requested and include the
  following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's name, address, telephone number and
    email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the applicant has a customs broker, the broker's
    name, address, telephone number and email address and the name of a
    contact person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the product name and UN number of each explosive to
    be imported;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each explosive to be imported or,
    if the application is for an annual permit, the estimated quantity
    of each explosive to be imported during the year;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the purpose for which each explosive is imported
    (personal, industrial or commercial use, reloading, field trial or
    other testing, sale, consignment, pyrotechnic event, fireworks
    display or other purpose);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who obtained the
    authorization of each explosive;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of entry through
    which each explosive will pass;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the address of the person to whom each explosive
    will be delivered and the location where it will be stored;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an explosive to be stored in a
    licensed factory or a licensed magazine, the number and expiry date
    of the factory or magazine licence and, for each explosive that will
    be stored there, the quantity of that explosive authorized by the
    licence to be stored;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an explosive to be stored in a
    magazine owned by a person other than the applicant, proof that the
    other person has agreed to have the explosive stored in their
    magazine; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the date of the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees for import permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An applicant for an import permit must pay the
  applicable fees set out in Part 19.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 6, 39
- SOR/2018-231, s. 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400067){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 13](#1451373-1452329){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451373-1452329.html"
  role="button"}

Version](section-46-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Requirements To Be Met by Import Permit Holder]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796054 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quantity of explosives and packaging

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of an import permit must ensure that the following requirements
  are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each explosive to be imported must
    not exceed the quantity of the explosive that the holder is
    authorized by a factory licence, a magazine licence or these
    Regulations to store; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the packaging in which each explosive is imported
    must conform to the description of the packaging set out in the
    explosive's authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on explosives

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an import permit must ensure that the
  following information is displayed on each explosive to be imported:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person who obtained the
    explosive's authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} either the date of its manufacture and the shift
    during which it was manufactured, if any, or its lot number;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its product name; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} instructions, in both English and French, for its
    safe handling, storage, use and destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of displaying information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an import permit must ensure that the
  information

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is legibly printed on the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is legibly printed on a label affixed to the
    explosive, if it is not possible to comply with paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is contained in a barcode or matrix code that is
    printed on the explosive, or on a label affixed to it, and can be
    read by a device that is available to the general public (for
    example, a smartphone), if it is not possible to comply with
    paragraph (a) and (b); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is legibly printed on the packaging containing the
    explosive or on a label affixed to the packaging, if it is not
    possible to comply with paragraphs (a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 2(d) does not apply in the case of
  fireworks that are imported for use at a special event, tour or
  international competition if the instructions are printed in a
  language understood by the person who will use the fireworks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on packaging

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an import permit must ensure that the
  following information is legibly printed on the packaging, or on a
  label affixed to the packaging, of each explosive to be imported:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words "Ammunition/[Munitions]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", "Explosives/[Explosifs]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Fireworks/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Pyrotechnics/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or
    "Rocket Motors/[Moteurs de fusée]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", or other
    descriptive wording, as the case may be, on the outer packaging and
    any inner packaging;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the product name of the explosive and the name and
    address of the person who obtained its authorization, on the outer
    packaging;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type F explosive, whether it is
    type F.1, F.2, F.3, F.4 or F.5, on the outer packaging; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type S explosive, whether it is
    type S.1 or S.2, on the outer packaging.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Printing deadline

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an import permit must ensure that the
  information to be printed on an explosive and its packaging is printed
  on them before the explosive is distributed and in any case no later
  than 15 days after the date on which the explosive is released under
  section 31 of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an import permit must complete, sign and
  send to the Chief Inspector of Explosives a report using the form
  provided by the Department of Natural Resources. The report must
  include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder's name, address, telephone number and
    email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder's permit number and its expiry date;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the product name and UN number of each explosive
    that was imported and the name of the person who obtained its
    authorization;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity and UN number of each type of
    explosive;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the means of transport used;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of entry through
    which each explosive passed; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the date of the report and the name of the person
    who completed the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- annual permit

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an annual permit must submit the report
  before the permit is renewed or, if the permit is not renewed, within
  a year after it expires. The report must be submitted even if no
  explosives were imported during the year for which the permit was
  valid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- single use permit

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a single use permit must submit the
  report within 30 days after the date on which the explosives were
  imported.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (7) to (9) do not apply to a holder that
  reports the importation of explosives by electronic means in
  accordance with section 4 of the [Reporting of Imported Goods
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-873).

::: HistoricalNote

- SOR/2018-231, ss. 9, 43, 44(F), 45(E)

### [Export Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796100 .Subheading}

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796101 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
export permit must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of
Explosives the application form provided by the Department of Natural
Resources. The application must state whether a single use permit or an
annual permit is requested and include the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's name, address, telephone number and
  email address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the applicant has a freight forwarder, their name,
  address, telephone number and email address and the name of a contact
  person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the product name and UN number of each explosive to
  be exported;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each explosive to be exported or, if
  the application is for an annual permit, the estimated quantity of
  each explosive to be exported during the year;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who obtained the authorization
  of each explosive;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of exit through
  which each explosive will pass;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email address
  of the person to whom each explosive will be delivered;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} a copy of a permit, or other proof, establishing that
  each explosive may lawfully enter the country of destination;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} a copy of a permit, or other proof, establishing that
  each explosive may lawfully transit any country through which it will
  be transported if the country requires permission for the in transit
  transportation of the explosive; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the date of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 8, 39
- SOR/2018-231, s. 44(F)

#### [Requirements To Be Met by Export Permit Holder]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796117 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on packaging

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of an export permit must ensure that the following information
  is legibly printed on the packaging, or on a label affixed to the
  packaging, of each explosive to be exported:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words "Ammunition/[Munitions]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", "Explosives/[Explosifs]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Fireworks/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Pyrotechnics/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or
    "Rocket Motors/[Moteurs de fusée]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", or other
    descriptive wording, as the case may be, on the outer packaging and
    any inner packaging;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the product name of the explosive and the name and
    address of the person who obtained its authorization, on the outer
    packaging;

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an export permit must complete, sign and
  send to the Chief Inspector of Explosives a report using the form
  provided by the Department of Natural Resources. The report must
  include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder's name, address, telephone number and
    email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder's permit number and its expiry date;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the product name and UN number of each explosive
    that was exported and the name of the person who obtained its
    authorization;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity and UN number of each type of
    explosive that was exported;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the means of transport used;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of exit through
    which each explosive passed; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the date of the report and the name of the person
    who completed the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- annual permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an annual permit must submit the report
  before the permit is renewed or, if the permit is not renewed, within
  a year after it expires. The report must be submitted even if no
  explosives were exported during the year for which the permit was
  valid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- single use permit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a single use permit must submit the
  report within 30 days after the date on which the explosives were
  exported.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) to (4) do not apply to a holder that
  reports the importation of explosives by electronic means in
  accordance with section 4 of the [Reporting of Imported Goods
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-86-873).

### [In Transit Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796141 .Subheading}

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796142 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an in
transit permit must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of
Explosives the application form provided by the Department of Natural
Resources. The application must state whether a single use permit or an
annual permit is requested and include the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's name, address, telephone number and
  email address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the applicant has a freight forwarder, their name,
  address, telephone number and email address and the name of a contact
  person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the product name and UN number of each explosive to
  be transported in transit;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an explosive to be transported is not on the list
  of authorized explosives referred to in subsection 41(1), the name or
  product name of the explosive, a description of the explosive and its
  UN number;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each explosive to be transported or,
  if the application is for an annual permit, the estimated quantity of
  each explosive to be transported during the year;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the name of the manufacturer of each explosive;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated dates of entry into and departure
  from Canada;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of entry and port
  of exit through which each explosive will pass;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the location in Canada of secure storage sites that
  may be used if the transportation is interrupted and, if any of the
  sites is a licensed factory or a licensed magazine, the number and
  expiry date of the factory or magazine licence and, for each explosive
  that might be stored there, the quantity of that explosive that is
  authorized by the licence to be stored;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} if any of the secure storage sites is a magazine that
  is owned by a person other than the applicant, proof that the other
  person has agreed to have the explosive stored in their magazine if an
  interruption in transportation occurs;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email address
  of the person to whom each explosive will be delivered;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} a copy of a permit, or other proof, establishing that
  each explosive may lawfully enter the country of destination;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} a copy of a permit, or other proof, establishing that
  each explosive may lawfully transit any country through which it will
  be transported if the country requires permission for the in transit
  transportation of the explosive; and

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} the date of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
