#### [Requirements To Be Met by In Transit Permit Holder]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796162 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on packaging

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of an in transit permit must ensure that the following
  information is legibly printed on the packaging, or on a label affixed
  to the packaging, of each explosive to be transported in transit:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name or product name of the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the explosive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the UN number of the explosive; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity to be transported.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interruption of in transit
  transportation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If anything interrupts an in transit transportation
  of explosives and, as a consequence, the explosives need to be stored,
  the holder of the in transit permit must ensure that they are stored
  in a licensed magazine, storage unit or dwelling in accordance with
  these Regulations or at a secure storage site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an in transit permit must complete, sign
  and send to the Chief Inspector of Explosives a report using the form
  provided by the Department of Natural Resources. The report must
  include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the permit holder's name, address, telephone number
    and email address;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder's permit number and its expiry date;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name or product name, the UN number and a
    description of each explosive that was transported in transit; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity and UN number of each type of
    explosive that was transported in transit;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the country of origin of each explosive;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the means of transport used;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the location of the Canadian port of entry and port
    of exit through which each explosive passed; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the date of the report and the name of the person
    who completed the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- annual permit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A holder of an annual permit must submit the report
  before the permit is renewed or, if the permit is not renewed, within
  a year after it expires. The report must be submitted even if no
  explosives were transported in transit during the year for which the
  permit was valid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline --- single use permit

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a single use permit must submit the
  report within 30 days after the date on which the explosives entered
  Canada.

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Manufacturing Explosives]{.HTitleText1} {#h-796188 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part sets out the rules for manufacturing explosives. However, it does
  not apply to the manufacture of small arms cartridges or black powder
  cartouches for personal use or to repackaging under a vendor magazine
  licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division 1

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Division 1 (sections 55 to 105) sets out how to
  obtain a division 1 factory licence or a satellite site certificate,
  as well as the requirements for holders of the licence or certificate
  and for workers at, and visitors to, the factory or satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division 2

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Division 2 (sections 106 to 132) sets out how to
  obtain a division 2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate, as
  well as the requirements for holders of the licence or certificate and
  for workers at, and visitors to, the workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division 3

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Division 3 (sections 133 to 142) sets out the
  manufacturing activities that do not require a factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate and the requirements for people who carry
  out those activities.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400092){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400092.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-52-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 52"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[manufacturing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[manufacturing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes the following activities:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} making or manufacturing an explosive substance from
  raw material or from another explosive substance;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} making or manufacturing an explosive article,
  including the assembly of an article from explosive and non-explosive
  components;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} altering or remaking an explosive substance or
  explosive article by modifying its chemical composition (for example,
  by gassing or blending) or by subjecting it to physical processes with
  the input of energy (for example, pneumatic handling, pumping,
  shearing or thickening);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} dividing an explosive into its components or
  unmaking, breaking up or in any manner destroying it;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} packaging explosives; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} testing an unauthorized explosive or testing an
  explosive to assess its potential for a use other than its authorized
  use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400098){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400098.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-53-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 53"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Explosive quantity

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** A reference to the mass
of an explosive in this Part is a reference to its net quantity (the
mass of the explosive excluding the mass of any packaging or container
and, in the case of an explosive article, also excluding any component
that is not an explosive substance).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400129){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400129.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-54-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 54"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Manufacturing Explosives under a Division 1 Factory Licence or a Satellite Site Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796208 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796209 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions --- sites and authorizations

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
relating to sites and authorizations apply in this Division.

[[client site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[client site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a blast site at which a
    mobile process unit is used to manufacture explosives away from a
    factory or satellite site. ([site client]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[division 1 factory licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[division 1 factory licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence
    that is issued under paragraph 7(1)(a) of the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and authorizes the manufacture of explosives at
    a factory. ([licence de fabrique de la section 1]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[satellite site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[satellite site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a site that is located
    away from a factory and at which explosives that are intended for
    use at a client site are manufactured and temporarily stored. ([site
    satellite]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[satellite site certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[satellite site certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    manufacturing certificate that is issued to the holder of a division
    1 factory licence under paragraph 7(1)(c) of the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and authorizes the manufacture of explosives at
    a satellite site. ([certificat de site satellite]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400140){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400140.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-55-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 55"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions --- facilities

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
relating to facilities and equipment at factories, satellite sites or
client sites apply in this Division.

[[factory magazine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[factory magazine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a magazine that is
    located at a factory or satellite site. ([poudrière de
    fabrique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[mobile process unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[mobile process unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vehicle or
    portable machine that is used at a factory, satellite site or client
    site to carry out an explosives manufacturing operation. ([unité de
    fabrication mobile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[process unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[process unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a building, structure,
    room or place in which an explosives manufacturing operation is
    carried out at a factory. ([unité de fabrication]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[raw material storage facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[raw material storage facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    facility where non-explosive raw material and packaging material are
    stored at a factory or satellite site. ([installation de stockage de
    matières premières]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[transport unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[transport unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a vehicle or container
    in which explosives or raw material are conveyed from one place to
    another at a factory or satellite site without using a public road.
    It includes a tow motor, forklift, wagon, handcart and basket, but
    does not include a conveyor or pipeline. ([unité de
    transport]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400144){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400144.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-56-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 56"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions --- people

**[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
relating to people at factories, satellite sites or client sites apply
in this Division.

[[competent person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[competent person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who has been
    certified as trained in accordance with section 83.  ([personne
    compétente]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[worker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[worker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is at a factory or
    satellite site to carry out a manufacturing operation or some other
    kind of work (for example, maintenance of facilities or repair of
    equipment) for the holder of a division 1 factory licence.
     ([travailleur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [SUBDIVISION a]{.HLabel3}[Authorized Activities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796227 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacture of explosives

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of a division 1 factory licence may manufacture explosives if
  the holder complies with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Type of explosive

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each explosive to be manufactured must be specified
  in the division 1 factory licence or a satellite site certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Place of manufacture

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Explosives must be manufactured at one of the
  following locations:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the factory specified in the division 1 factory
    licence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a satellite site specified in a satellite site
    certificate; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a client site specified in the licence or a
    certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturing operations and work

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A manufacturing operation may be carried out in a
  process unit or factory magazine, and maintenance and other work may
  be done to a process unit, factory magazine, raw material storage
  facility or transport unit, only if the manufacturing operation,
  maintenance or other work is specified in the division 1 factory
  licence or a satellite site certificate. However, the minor servicing
  authorized by these Regulations does not have to be specified in the
  licence or certificate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition, storage and sale of
explosives

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of a division 1 factory licence may acquire and store
  explosives and may sell explosives without a vendor magazine licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with Parts 10 to 18

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder who acquires or sells explosives must comply
  with Parts 10 to 18. However, they are not required to comply with the
  provisions of those Parts that apply to the storage of explosives if
  the explosives are stored at the factory or a satellite site.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400160){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400160.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION b]{.HLabel3}[Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796245 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant for a division 1 factory licence or a satellite site
  certificate must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of
  Explosives the application form provided by the Department of Natural
  Resources. The application must state whether a licence or a
  certificate is requested and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email
    address of both the applicant and a contact person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each type of explosive to be manufactured;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the application is for a satellite site
    certificate, the number of the applicant's division 1 factory
    licence and the dates on which the operations at the satellite site
    will begin and end;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the address of the factory or satellite site; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email
    address of a contact person at the factory or satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans and sketches

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a factory site and area plan or a satellite site
    and area plan that shows

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the topography of the site,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the location of every process unit, factory
      magazine and raw material storage facility, and every building or
      structure that contains such a unit, magazine or facility,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the location of any other building or
      structure, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between each process
      unit, factory magazine, raw material storage facility, building
      and structure;

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the area surrounding the site that is exposed to
      the hazards (for example, debris or blast effect) that could
      result from an ignition of the explosives to be manufactured or
      stored at the site,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} each vulnerable place within that area, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between each vulnerable
      place and each process unit, factory magazine and raw material
      storage facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} layout sketches, diagrams or drawings that show

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the workplace areas, storage areas and emergency
      exits of every process unit, factory magazine, raw material
      storage facility and every building or structure that contains
      such a unit, magazine or facility,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the equipment to be used in each process unit,
      including piping and instrumentation diagrams and equipment layout
      drawings, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the manufacturing operations to be carried out,
      including process flow sheets or process schematic drawings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Site description

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following
  information about the factory or satellite site:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its geographical coordinates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the size of, and construction materials used for,
    each building at the site and all lighting, heating, ventilation and
    air conditioning systems, electrical installations, grounding and
    measures for protection from fire and lightning;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of all site and building security
    features (for example, fencing, barriers and warning signs);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the principal manufacturing
    equipment to be used and its safety features;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of each mobile process unit to be
    used;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of any other mobile equipment,
    including transport units, to be used and how the equipment will be
    powered; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a description of any safety features at the site to
    be put in place to minimize the likelihood of harm to people or
    property (for example, diking, sumps, blowout panels, means of blast
    containment, barriers, alarms or pressure relief and control
    systems).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Client site

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the manufacture of explosives is to be carried out
  at a client site, the application must include the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email
    address of a contact person at the client site;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the client site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the distance in kilometres between the factory and
    the client site; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the distance in kilometres between any satellite
    site and the client site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Explosives description

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a description that sets
  out,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each explosive to be manufactured at the
    factory, a satellite site or a client site,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its product name and its UN proper shipping name,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the explosive was authorized
      or its authorization file number,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} its UN number, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} its hazard category; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for each explosive to be stored at the factory or a
    satellite site,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its UN proper shipping name,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} its UN number, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} its hazard category.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturing operations description

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following
  information about manufacturing operations:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the operations to be carried out
    in each process unit and factory magazine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the explosives, and of any other
    thing that is flammable, that is liable to spontaneously combust or
    that is otherwise dangerous, that will be stored in each process
    unit, factory magazine, raw material storage facility, building and
    structure;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the results of a quantified risk assessment or
    hazard operability review of any manufacturing operation to be used
    to manufacture an explosive if that operation has not previously
    been used in Canada to manufacture that explosive;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the maximum quantity of explosives and raw material
    that will be in each process unit, factory magazine, raw material
    storage facility, building and structure at any one time;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the maximum number of people who will be in each
    process unit, factory magazine, raw material storage facility,
    building and structure at any one time; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the minimum distance in metres that must be
    maintained between each process unit, factory magazine and raw
    material storage facility and each vulnerable place shown on the
    area plan, as set out in the National Standard of Canada Standard
    CAN/BNQ 2910--510/2015 entitled Explosives --- Quantity Distances as
    amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security plan

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If type E, I or D explosives are to be manufactured
  or stored, the application must include a security plan that includes
  the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an assessment of the security risks resulting from
    the presence of the explosives at the factory, satellite site or
    client site;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the measures that will be taken to
    minimize those risks;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the procedures that will be
    followed to respond to security incidents; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the procedures that will be
    followed to report security incidents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of documents

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a list of the following
  documents, along with the dates on which they were made and the dates
  of any amendments:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any environmental assessment of the factory,
    satellite site or client site or of the operations to be carried out
    there;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the documents that set out the rules, procedures
    and protocols designed to ensure compliance with the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17), these Regulations and the licence, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} operating procedures,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} maintenance procedures,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} training manuals,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} emergency response plans, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} spill contingency plans;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if manufacturing operations are to be carried out
    in a quarry, a letter of understanding signed by the quarry operator
    that sets out the safety measures that will be taken at the quarry;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if activities that could increase the likelihood of
    an ignition (for example, welding) are to be carried out, a document
    that sets out the rules governing those activities; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if explosives are to be destroyed, a document that
    sets out the method of destruction to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identifier

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every process unit, factory magazine, raw material
  storage facility, building and structure that is shown on the site
  plan and every vulnerable place that is shown on the area plan must be
  identified by a number, letter or distinctive name, which must be used
  to identify the unit, magazine, building or structure in every
  drawing, sketch or description included in the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scale drawing

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Every drawing, sketch or plan must be drawn to
  scale, or be a reasonable approximation of actual distances and
  dimensions, and must include a legend.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
division 1 factory licence or a satellite site certificate must pay the
applicable fees set out in Part 19.

#### [SUBDIVISION c]{.HLabel3}[Requirements for Holders of Division 1 Factory Licences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796328 .Subheading}

##### [Facilities at Factories and Satellite Sites]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796329 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the requirements relating to process
units, factory magazines, raw material storage facilities and transport
units set out in sections 63 to 68 are met at the factory and, if they
also hold a satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptable distance requirement

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  process unit, factory magazine and raw material storage facility must
  be located at an acceptable distance from surrounding structures and
  infrastructure and from places where people are likely to be present.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria --- acceptable distance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Acceptable distance is determined by the Minister on
  the basis of risk of harm to people or property, taking into account
  the quantity and type of explosives that are to be manufactured, the
  raw material to be used, the manufacturing operations to be carried
  out, the strength, proximity and use of surrounding structures and
  infrastructure and the number of people likely to be in the vicinity
  of the unit, magazine or facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Structural requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every process unit, factory magazine and transport
  unit must be designed, constructed and installed to conform to good
  engineering practices. It must be constructed in a manner that
  prevents the accumulation of explosives or raw material in cracks and
  cavities and that minimizes the harm to people and property that could
  result from an ignition of the explosives or raw material. The
  construction materials must be compatible with the explosives to be
  manufactured, stored or transported and with the raw material to be
  used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factory magazines

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every factory magazine must also be constructed so
  that it is well ventilated and resistant to theft, weather and fire.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Raw material storage facilities

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every raw material storage facility must be designed,
  constructed and installed to conform to good engineering practices.
  The construction materials must be compatible with the raw material to
  be stored in the facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means of escape

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every process unit, factory magazine, raw material
  storage facility and transport unit must have a means of escape that
  will permit all people in the unit, magazine or facility to leave it
  quickly and easily in an emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lighting, electrical fixtures and
  wiring systems

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The lighting, electrical fixtures and wiring systems
  that are used in a process unit, factory magazine, raw material
  storage facility or transport unit must not increase the likelihood of
  an accidental ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electrostatic hazard

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Precautions that eliminate any possibility of an
  accidental ignition of electrostatic sensitive material (for example,
  grounding and control of humidity) must be taken in every process
  unit, factory magazine, raw material storage facility and transport
  unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection against lightning strikes

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every process unit or building in which a process
  unit is located must be protected from lightning strikes if it would
  not be safe to shut down manufacturing operations in the process unit
  during a thunderstorm.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 38
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400175){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400175.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20160415.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equipment

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  tools, accessories and equipment that are in a process unit, factory
  magazine, raw material storage facility or transport unit must be
  designed, constructed and installed to conform to good engineering
  practices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compatibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every thing that is in a process unit, factory
  magazine, raw material storage facility or transport unit must be made
  from materials that are compatible with the explosives and raw
  material in the unit, magazine or facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incompatible things

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} However, a thing that is not compatible with an
  explosive or raw material in a process unit, factory magazine or raw
  material storage facility but is required for manufacturing or
  maintenance (such as a cleaning fluid or solvent) may be brought into
  the unit, magazine or facility for immediate use. It must be removed
  as soon as the circumstances permit after its use unless the division
  1 factory licence or satellite site certificate authorizes its storage
  in the unit, magazine or facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control of safety-critical parameters

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All equipment designed to control safety-critical
  parameters on pumps, and on other process equipment that puts energy
  into explosives, must be installed and maintained in good condition so
  that the equipment operates while the pumps and process equipment are
  in use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Motorized transport units

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A motorized transport unit that contains explosives
  must be equipped with two easily accessible fire extinguishers, each
  with a rating of at least 4-A :40-B:C.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Open-flame device

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An open-flame device, an open-element electrical
  appliance or a device (for example, a heat sealer) with a surface
  temperature above the decomposition temperature of the explosives or
  raw material with which it may come in contact must not be stored in a
  process unit unless the division 1 factory licence or satellite site
  certificate authorizes one to be stored in the unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400180){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400180.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-64-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 64"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Containers

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  raw material, explosive substances and explosives waste must be kept
  in closed containers that prevent spills and contamination. The
  contents of each container must be clearly identified on a label
  attached to the container.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Spills

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any spill of explosives, raw material or other
  material must be cleaned up as soon as the circumstances permit so as
  to eliminate any possibility of an ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign matter

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the likelihood of an ignition could be increased
  as a result of foreign matter (for example, bolts, gravel or grit)
  being present in or mixed with any raw material, or explosive used as
  raw material, the material must be carefully examined and passed
  through a sieve or treated to remove or exclude the foreign matter
  before manufacturing operations begin.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of explosives from process unit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All explosives and raw material must be removed from
  a process unit as soon as the circumstances permit after manufacturing
  operations are completed, unless the division 1 factory licence or
  satellite site certificate authorizes storage of the explosives or raw
  material in the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of explosives from transport
  unit

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} All explosives and raw material must be removed from
  a transport unit as soon as the circumstances permit after their
  transport is completed. A transport unit that contains explosives must
  be attended in person. However, it may be left unattended during a
  thunderstorm.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of waste and contaminated
  material

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} All explosives waste and explosives-contaminated
  material must be destroyed in a manner that does not increase the
  likelihood of an accidental ignition during or after the destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Any building or equipment that is no longer being
  used to manufacture explosives must be decontaminated as soon as the
  circumstances permit. The building or equipment must be inspected by a
  supervisor to verify that it no longer contains any explosives.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400188){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400188.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Process unit records

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record must be made for each period during which a process unit
  operates. The record must be kept for two years after the date on
  which it is made and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each unit,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the dates on which the period of its operation
      began and ended,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a short description of the explosives, and the
      properties of the explosives, manufactured during the period,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of explosives manufactured, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of and repairs to the unit, the
      dates on which the work was carried out and the name of the worker
      who did the work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for any equipment in the unit whose malfunction
    could increase the likelihood of an ignition,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the dates on which the period of its operation
      began and ended,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the manufacturing operations for which the
      equipment was used, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of and repairs to the
      equipment, the dates on which the work was carried out and the
      name of the person who did the work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination records

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record must be made for each decontamination of
  equipment that was used in a process unit. The record must describe
  the contamination and the means of decontamination and include the
  names of the workers who carried out the decontamination and the name
  of the supervisor who inspected the equipment after the
  decontamination. The record must be kept for two years after the date
  on which the equipment is disposed of.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400194){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400194.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Thunderstorms

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the
  approach of and during a thunderstorm, the following procedures must
  be followed:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all manufacturing operations in a process unit that
    can be safely shut down must be shut down;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all entrances to any factory magazine containing
    explosives must be closed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any transport unit containing explosives must be
    immediately moved to an isolated safe place; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all people at the factory or satellite site must be
    immediately moved to a safe place and, until the storm passes, must
    not be permitted to return.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Process units

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, any manufacturing operation in a process
  unit that could, if stopped, increase the likelihood of harm to people
  or property may be carried on during a thunderstorm until the
  operation can be safely shut down.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400198){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400198.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  process unit, factory magazine and transport unit and all equipment
  that is used to manufacture explosives must be maintained in good
  operating condition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Servicing during use

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Minor servicing of a process unit, factory magazine
  or transport unit, or of equipment that is used to manufacture
  explosives, may be done while the unit, magazine or equipment is in
  use if the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the servicing is routine and is integral to the use
    of the unit, magazine or equipment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the servicing is done by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All other maintenance or repair work that is done at
  the factory or a satellite site in or to a process unit, factory
  magazine, transport unit or equipment that is used to manufacture
  explosives must be done by a competent person who has been issued a
  work permit by the licence holder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of work permit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A work permit must set out the procedures to be
  followed during the maintenance or repair work and the measures that
  must be taken before, during and after the work to eliminate any
  possibility of an ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If any transport unit or equipment at the factory or
  a satellite site is contaminated, it must be decontaminated at the
  factory before it is taken outside the factory for maintenance, repair
  or disposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work done outside factory or satellite
  site

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If maintenance or repair work is done outside the
  factory or a satellite site on manufacturing equipment whose
  malfunction could increase the likelihood of an ignition (for example,
  pumps or safety trips), it must be done by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Logbook and work permits

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A logbook of all maintenance and repair work done in
  or to every process unit, factory magazine and transport unit, and to
  any manufacturing equipment whose malfunction could increase the
  likelihood of an ignition, must be kept for two years after the date
  on which the last entry is made. The work permits for this maintenance
  and repair work must also be kept for two years after the date on
  which the work is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progressive cavity pump logbook

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For each progressive cavity pump, a separate logbook
  that sets out the operating history of the pump and the maintenance
  and repair work done to it must be kept at the factory for the life of
  the pump.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400204){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400204.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-68-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Signs]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796423 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the signage requirements set out in
sections 70 to 72 are met at the factory and, if they also hold a
satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs

**[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** A sign that warns against
unauthorized entry must be posted at each entrance to a factory or
satellite site in a clearly visible location. The sign must also warn of
danger from explosives and indicate the precautions that must be taken
to eliminate the possibility of an accidental ignition.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400208){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400208.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exterior signs --- process units and
factory magazines

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the
  outside of each process unit and factory magazine, a sign that sets
  out either the number, letter or distinctive name specified in the
  division 1 factory licence or satellite site certificate for that unit
  or magazine or the activities for which the unit or magazine is used,
  must be posted at each entrance in a clearly visible location.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interior signs --- process units and
  factory magazines

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the inside of each process unit and factory
  magazine, a sign that sets out the following information must be
  posted at the main entrance in a clearly visible location:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each type of explosive and the
    quantity of raw material that are authorized to be in the unit or
    magazine at any one time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of people who are authorized to be in
    the unit or magazine at any one time; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other conditions or restrictions specified in
    the licence or certificate that apply to the unit or magazine.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400212){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400212.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-71-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 71"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interior signs --- raw material storage
facilities

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** On the inside of each raw
material storage facility, a sign that sets out the raw material, and
the properties of the raw material, that is authorized to be stored in
the facility, and the quantity of raw material that is authorized to be
stored at any one time, must be posted at the main entrance in a clearly
visible location. The sign must also indicate the precautions that must
be taken to eliminate the possibility of an ignition of the raw
materials.

##### [Labelling]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796438 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the labelling requirements set out in
section 74 are met at the factory and, if they also hold a satellite
site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on explosives

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following information must be displayed on each explosive that is
  manufactured at the factory or satellite site:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person who obtained the
    explosive's authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} either the date of its manufacture and the shift
    during which it was manufactured, if any, or its lot number;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its product name; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} instructions, in both English and French, for its
    safe handling, storage, use and destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of displaying information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The information must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} legibly printed on the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} legibly printed on a label affixed to the
    explosive, if it is not possible to comply with paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} contained in a barcode or matrix code that is
    printed on the explosive, or on a label affixed to it, and can be
    read by a device that is available to the general public (for
    example, a smartphone), if it is not possible to comply with
    paragraphs (a) and (b); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} legibly printed on the packaging containing the
    explosive or on a label affixed to the packaging, if it is not
    possible to comply with paragraphs (a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on packaging

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following information must be legibly printed on
  the packaging, or on a label affixed to the packaging, of the
  explosive:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words "Ammunition/[Munitions]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", "Explosives/[Explosifs]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Fireworks/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Pyrotechnics/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or
    "Rocket Motors/[Moteurs de fusée]{.otherLang lang="fr"}", or other
    descriptive wording, as the case may be, on the outer packaging and
    any inner packaging;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the product name of the explosive and the name and
    address of the person who obtained its authorization, on the outer
    packaging;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type F explosive, whether it is
    type F.1, F.2, F.3, F.4 or F.5, on the outer packaging; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type S explosive, whether it is
    type S.1 or S.2, on the outer packaging.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on industrial explosives

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The manufacturer's division 1 factory licence number
  must be printed in a legible and permanent manner on the outer
  packaging or container of every industrial explosive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the outer packaging or containers of industrial
    explosives if the number is contained in a barcode or matrix code
    that is printed on the packaging or container;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} containers holding industrial explosives in bulk;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} intermediate bulk containers holding industrial
    explosives in bulk; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} plastic bags that meet the requirements of UN
    packaging code 5H1, 5H2, 5H3 or 5H4.

::: HistoricalNote

  role="button"}

##### [Safety of Workers and Visitors]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796464 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the worker and visitor safety
requirements set out in sections 76 to 79 are met at the factory and, if
they also hold a satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Only
  people authorized by the holder of a division 1 factory licence may
  have access to the factory or a satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orientation sessions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A visitor may be authorized to enter the factory or a
  satellite site only if they have attended an orientation session on
  visitor safety within the 12 months before the date on which they
  enter. If any of the safety procedures have changed since their last
  orientation session, they must attend a new session before their entry
  is authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visitors --- general public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A visitor may be authorized to enter any part of the
  factory or a satellite site if they are at least 17 years old and
  remain under the supervision of a competent person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lists of personal protective equipment

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Up-to-date lists of the personal
  protective equipment, clothing and devices that are needed to protect
  workers and visitors from the hazards to which they could be exposed
  while at the factory or a satellite site must be maintained and must
  be made available to all workers and visitors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal protective equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor must be required to wear the
  personal protective equipment, clothing and devices that are needed to
  protect them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hair, clothing and accessories

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor must be required to confine
  or cover any loose hair and confine, cover or remove any loose
  clothing, jewellery or other accessories if the hair, clothing or
  accessories could increase the likelihood of an ignition or the
  likelihood of harm to the worker or visitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic devices

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor must be required to
  deactivate any electronic device in their possession (for example, a
  cellphone or two-way radio) in any part of the factory or a satellite
  site where the device, if activated, could increase the likelihood of
  an ignition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** A worker or visitor must
not be authorized to enter the factory or a satellite site if there are
reasonable grounds to believe that they are under the influence of or
carrying alcohol or another performance-diminishing substance. However,
a worker or visitor who has taken a prescription drug may be permitted
to enter if they have medical proof that the drug is needed and that it
will not impede their ability to carry out their functions or to visit
safely.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No smoking

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Smoking must be prohibited at the
  factory and any satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No fire-producing devices

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Fire-producing devices (for example, matches and
  lighters) must not be permitted in the factory or any satellite site
  unless they are authorized by the division 1 factory licence or
  satellite site certificate.

##### [Training]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796490 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the training requirements for workers
set out in sections 81 to 84 are met at the factory and, if they also
hold a satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employees' qualifications

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** Every employee at a
factory or satellite site must be required to meet the following
requirements:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} they must be a competent person; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} they must be at least 18 years old, participating in
  the training program referred to in section 82 and under the direct
  supervision of a competent person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 21](#1451413-1452364){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451413-1452364.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training program

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employee must be trained to carry out their duties at the factory or a
  satellite site in a safe and lawful manner. The training must be given
  by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of training

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The training must include all information necessary
  to ensure the security of the factory or satellite site and the safety
  of the employee, other people at the factory or site and the general
  public. It must also include a review of the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17), these Regulations and the division 1 factory
  licence or satellite site certificate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of a division 1 factory licence must certify as trained any
  employee who is at least 18 years old if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee has completed the training referred to
    in section 82; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder has reasonable grounds to believe that
    the employee understands the hazards to which they could be exposed
    at the factory or satellite site and is competent to carry out their
    duties in a manner that is safe, lawful and ensures the security of
    the site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Evidence of an employee's certification may be either
  a training record or a document signed by the person who provided the
  training or, in the case provided for in paragraph (1)(b), by the
  holder of the division 1 factory licence. It must be given to the
  employee and record the employee's name, the operating procedures the
  employee is competent to carry out and the date on which the
  certification will expire.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee's training certification must expire not
  more than five years after the date of the certification. If a change
  occurs in the operating procedures for which the employee was
  certified, the employee must be trained in the new operating
  procedures but the expiry date of the certification must remain the
  same.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A training record and a record of work experience
  must be made and kept up to date for each employee at the factory or
  satellite site where they perform their duties. These records must be
  kept for two years after the date on which the employee's
  certification expires.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training and supervision

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** Every worker at a factory
or satellite site who is not an employee must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} be trained to carry out their work in a manner that
  is safe and lawful and ensures the security of the factory or
  satellite site; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} remain under the supervision of a competent person
  while at the factory or satellite site, unless the holder of the
  division 1 factory licence has reasonable grounds to believe that the
  worker understands the hazards to which the worker could be exposed
  and is competent to carry out their duties in a manner that is safe,
  lawful and ensures the security of the factory or site.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-84-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 84"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Operation of the Factory or Satellite Site]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796519 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the operational requirements set out in
sections 86 to 91 are met at the factory and, if they also hold a
satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating procedures

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Operating procedures, including
  procedures to minimize the likelihood of an accidental ignition, must
  be put in place for every manufacturing operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Update of procedures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operating procedures must be kept up to date and
  reviewed annually. If a manufacturing operation is to be changed, the
  procedures for carrying out that operation must be reviewed and
  modified as needed before the change is implemented.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management of change

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before
  any temporary or permanent technological or organizational change to
  manufacturing operations is implemented, an evaluation of the risk
  that the change could increase the likelihood of an ignition or a
  security incident must be carried out and a management of change
  procedure must be put in place for each change that could increase the
  likelihood of an ignition or a security incident.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of change

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each change must be approved by the holder of the
  division 1 factory licence before it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A record of each change must be kept for two years
  after the date on which the change is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security plan

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  security plan was included in the application for a division 1 factory
  licence or a satellite site certificate, it must be implemented and a
  copy of the plan must be kept at the factory or satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of circumstance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The security plan must be updated to reflect any
  change in circumstances that could adversely affect the security of
  the factory or satellite site. A copy of the updated plan must be kept
  at the factory and satellite site and another copy must be sent to the
  Chief Inspector of Explosives as soon as the circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the most recent version of the plan must be
  made available to the people who are responsible for implementing it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audits

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  operating procedures must be routinely audited. Every audit must
  include the following verifications:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether facilities and equipment are being operated
    and maintained in a manner that ensures the security of the factory
    or satellite site and minimizes the likelihood of harm to people and
    property; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether these Regulations and the terms and
    conditions of the division 1 factory licence and any satellite site
    certificate are being complied with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrections

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any deficiency found during an audit must be
  corrected as soon as the circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A record of each audit must be made and the record
  must include a copy of the action plan to correct any deficiencies
  found and set out the corrections made. It must be kept for two years
  after the date on which the audit is conducted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit procedure

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A procedure must be put in place to ensure that the
  audits are conducted in a complete and timely manner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record of each explosive in the factory or at a satellite site
  (including any explosive that is used as raw material) must be kept
  for two years after the date on which the record is made. It must set
  out, for each explosive,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product name, UN number and a short description
    of the explosive;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was acquired, the quantity
    acquired and the date of acquisition;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was used to manufacture another
    explosive, the quantity used and the date of use;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} if the manufactured explosive referred to in
    paragraph (c) was used at the factory or a satellite site or client
    site, the quantity used and the date of use;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was manufactured at the factory or
    a satellite site, the quantity manufactured and the date of
    manufacture;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was stored, the quantity stored,
    the magazine in which it was stored and the dates on which it was
    placed in and removed from the magazine;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was shipped from the factory or a
    satellite site, the quantity shipped, the date of shipment and the
    name and address of the person to whom it was shipped; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was destroyed at the factory, the
    quantity destroyed and the date of destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inventory control systems

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any system used for inventory control or tracking of
  an explosive must not increase the likelihood of an ignition.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 39
- SOR/2018-231, s. 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400221){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451418-1452368.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-90-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- licence and certificates

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A copy
  of the division 1 factory licence and all documents referred to in the
  licence must be kept at the factory. A copy of the satellite site
  certificate and all documents referred to in the certificate must be
  kept at each satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- this Division

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of this Division must be made available to
  workers at the factory and each satellite site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- operating procedures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the operating procedures for each process
  unit, factory magazine and transport unit must be kept in the unit or
  magazine to which they relate and must be made available to the
  workers.

##### [Mobile Process Units]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796573 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of licence holder

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division 1
factory licence must ensure that the mobile process unit requirements
set out in sections 93 to 100 are met at the factory and, if they also
hold a satellite site certificate, at each satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Structural requirements

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  mobile process unit must be designed and constructed to conform to
  good engineering practices. It must be constructed in a manner that
  prevents the accumulation of explosives or raw material in cracks and
  cavities and that minimizes the harm to people and property that could
  result from an ignition of the explosives or raw material. The
  construction materials must be compatible with the explosives to be
  manufactured and transported and with the raw material to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means of escape

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every mobile process unit must have a means of escape
  that will permit all people in the unit to leave it quickly and easily
  in an emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equipment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All tools, equipment, hoses and hydraulic systems in
  or on a mobile process unit must be designed, constructed and
  installed to conform to good engineering practices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compatibility

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every thing that is in or on a mobile process unit
  must be made from materials that are compatible with the explosives
  and raw material with which they could come into contact during
  manufacturing operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control of safety-critical parameters

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} All equipment designed to control safety-critical
  parameters on pumps, and on other process equipment that puts energy
  into explosives, must be installed and maintained in good condition so
  that the equipment operates while the pumps and process equipment are
  in use.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400225){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400225.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-93-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign matter

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  likelihood of an ignition could be increased as a result of foreign
  matter (for example, bolts, gravel or grit) being present in or mixed
  with any raw material, or explosive used as raw material, the material
  must be carefully examined and passed through a sieve or treated to
  remove or exclude the foreign matter before manufacturing operations
  begin.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loading and unloading

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Explosives and raw material must not be loaded into
  or unloaded from a mobile process unit except at the factory or a
  satellite site or at a client site specified in the division 1 factory
  licence or satellite site certificate. However, in the case of a
  mechanical breakdown, unloading may be carried out at the location of
  the breakdown if the Minister is notified of the breakdown and
  determines that precautions that minimize the likelihood of ignition
  have been taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loaded units to be attended

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A mobile process unit that contains explosives must
  be attended in person except when it is at the factory or a satellite
  site or when it is awaiting towing or repair and is secured in an
  access-controlled mine site or quarry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage --- contaminated units

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A mobile process unit that has not been
  decontaminated must be kept at the location specified in the licence
  or certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tanks and hoppers

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The explosives and raw material in the tanks and
  hoppers on a mobile process unit must be unloaded if the unit will not
  be used for three consecutive days. However, the fuel oil tank and
  prill hopper do not have to be unloaded.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 38
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400232){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400232.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 23](#1451423-1452371){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451423-1452371.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-94-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unit unused

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  mobile process unit must be cleaned if it will not be used, or has not
  been used, for 30 consecutive days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal from service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A mobile process unit must be decontaminated before
  it is removed from service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All decontamination of a mobile process unit must be
  carried out at the factory specified in the division 1 factory
  licence. After decontamination, a unit must be inspected by a
  supervisor to verify that it no longer contains any explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of waste

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} All explosives waste and explosives-contaminated
  material must be destroyed in a manner that does not increase the
  likelihood of an accidental ignition during or after the destruction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400237){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400237.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-95-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 95"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating procedures

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Operating procedures, including
  procedures to minimize the likelihood of an ignition, must be put in
  place for every manufacturing operation to be carried out in a mobile
  process unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the operating procedures for each mobile
  process unit must be kept in the unit and must be made available to
  workers.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Process unit records

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  record must be made for each period during which a mobile process unit
  operates. The record must be kept for two years after the date on
  which it is made and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the dates on which the period of its operation
    began and ended;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a short description of the explosives manufactured;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of explosives manufactured; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of and repairs to the unit, the
    dates on which the work was carried out and the name of the person
    who did the work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination records

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A record must be made for each decontamination of
  equipment that is used in a mobile process unit. The record must
  describe the contamination and the means of decontamination and
  include the names of the workers who carried out the decontamination
  and the name of the supervisor who inspected the equipment after the
  decontamination. The record must be kept for two years after the date
  on which the equipment is disposed of.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400243){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400243.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 24](#1451425-1452373){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451425-1452373.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-97-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 97"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance

- **[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  mobile process unit must be maintained in good operating condition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Servicing during use

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Minor servicing of a mobile process unit may be done
  while it is in use if the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the servicing is routine and integral to the use of
    the unit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the servicing is done by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All other maintenance or repair work, if it is done
  at the factory, must be carried out by a competent person who has been
  issued a work permit by the licence holder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of work permit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A work permit must set out the procedures to be
  followed during the maintenance or repair work and the measures that
  must be taken before, during and after the work to eliminate any
  possibility of an ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A mobile process unit must be decontaminated at the
  factory before it is taken outside the factory for maintenance or
  repair.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work done outside factory

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If maintenance or repair work is done outside the
  factory on manufacturing equipment that is in or on a mobile process
  unit and whose malfunction could increase the likelihood of an
  ignition (for example, pumps or safety trips), it must be done by a
  competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repairs at site of breakdown

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a mechanical breakdown of a vehicle
  used as a mobile process unit, repairs to the vehicle may be done at
  the location of the breakdown only if doing the repair work will not
  increase the likelihood of an ignition and the work is done by a
  competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unloading and towing

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If doing the repair work could increase the
  likelihood of ignition, the unit must be towed to the factory for
  repair.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance record and work permits

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A maintenance record of all maintenance and repair
  work done to a mobile process unit, or to any manufacturing equipment
  in or on the unit whose malfunction could increase the likelihood of
  an ignition, must be kept for two years after the date on which the
  last entry is made. The work permits for the maintenance and repair
  work must also be kept for two years after the date on which the work
  is completed. The maintenance record and permits must be kept at the
  factory.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progressive cavity pump logbook and
  maintenance record

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For each progressive cavity pump, a separate logbook
  that sets out the operating history of the pump and a separate
  maintenance record that sets out the maintenance and repair work done
  to it must be kept at the factory for the life of the pump.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400248){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400248.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 25](#1451427-1452378){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451427-1452378.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-98-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 98"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use at client site

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  mobile process unit may be used to manufacture explosives at a client
  site, but only if the unit and the site are both specified in the
  division 1 factory licence or satellite site certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturing operations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All manufacturing operations at a client site must be
  carried out by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Precautions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before manufacturing operations begin, every one at
  the client site must be informed of the precautions that must be taken
  while dewatering, driving over loaded boreholes, handling charging
  hose and carrying out charging operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hazards

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any thing or activity that could increase the
  likelihood of an accidental ignition must not be allowed within 15 m
  of a mobile process unit and its charging hose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person must not be permitted to be within 15 m of a
  mobile process unit and its charging hose while the unit is operating
  if there are reasonable grounds to believe that the person is under
  the influence of or is carrying alcohol or another
  performance-diminishing substance. A person who has taken a
  prescription drug may be permitted to enter the area if they have
  medical proof that the drug is needed and will not impede their
  ability to function safely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No smoking

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person must not be permitted to smoke within 15 m
  of a mobile process unit and its charging hose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Thunderstorms

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On the approach of a thunderstorm, if a mobile
  process unit is at the surface at a client site, all manufacturing
  operations in the unit must be shut down and all people in the
  vicinity of the unit must be immediately moved to a safe place. Until
  the storm passes, the operations must remain shut down and the people
  must not be permitted to return to the vicinity of the unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400252){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400252.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Packaging at satellite or client site

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Blasting explosives in bulk that are
  manufactured in a mobile process unit may be packaged at a satellite
  or client site, but only if the explosives

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have been removed to prepare the unit for cleaning
    or decontamination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are to be used as samples for laboratory analysis;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have been used to calibrate the process equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} are to be used to charge boreholes that the unit
    cannot access during its operations at a client site; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} are to be used to charge boreholes that present
    unforeseen conditions rendering their use unsafe if they are left
    unpackaged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Packaging

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The packaging must prevent the explosives from
  leaking or spilling and minimize the likelihood of an accidental
  ignition.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400256){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400256.html"
  role="button"}
:::
