[Previous
Version](section-100-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 100"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION d]{.HLabel3}[Requirements for Workers, Visitors and Other People]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796671 .Subheading}

##### [Factory and Satellite Site]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796672 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization to enter

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before entering a factory or
  satellite site, every worker who is not an employee and every visitor
  must obtain the authorization of the holder of the division 1 factory
  licence. A visitor must be at least 17 years old.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visitors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} While at a factory or satellite site, a visitor must
  remain under the supervision of a competent person, unless the holder
  has reasonable grounds to believe that the visitor understands the
  hazards to which the visitor could be exposed and is competent to
  carry out their duties in a manner that is safe, lawful and ensures
  the security of the factory or site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A worker or visitor who is under the influence of or
  is carrying alcohol or another performance-diminishing substance must
  not enter a factory or satellite site. However, a worker or visitor
  who has taken a prescription drug may enter if they have medical proof
  that they need the drug and that it will not impede their ability to
  carry out their functions or to visit safely.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal protective equipment

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor at a
  factory or satellite site must wear the personal protective equipment,
  clothing and devices that are needed to protect them from the hazards
  to which they could be exposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hair, clothing and accessories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor must confine or cover any
  loose hair and confine, cover or remove any loose clothing, jewelry or
  other accessories if the hair, clothing or accessories could increase
  the likelihood of an ignition or the likelihood of harm to the worker
  or visitor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic devices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor must deactivate any
  electronic device in their possession (for example, a cellphone or
  two-way radio) in any part of a factory or satellite site where the
  device, if activated, could increase the likelihood of an ignition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Precautions

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every worker and visitor at a
  factory or satellite site must take the precautions that they are
  directed to take to minimize the likelihood of an accidental ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No smoking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Workers and visitors must not smoke at a factory or
  satellite site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Worker qualifications

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** A worker at a factory
or satellite site may carry out a task only if they

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} have been trained in that task and understand the
  hazards to which they could be exposed; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} are at least 18 years old, participating in a
  training program and under the direct supervision of a competent
  person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 26](#1451429-1452381){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451429-1452381.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 104"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Client Sites]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796698 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person at a client site must not be within 15 m of an operating mobile
  process unit and its charging hose if they are under the influence of
  or are carrying alcohol or another performance-diminishing substance.
  However, a person who has taken a prescription drug may enter the area
  if they have medical proof that they need the drug and that it will
  not impede their ability to function safely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hazards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person at a client site must not be in possession
  of any thing, or carry out any activity, within 15 m of a mobile
  process unit and its charging hose if the thing or activity could
  increase the likelihood of an accidental ignition.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Manufacturing Explosives under a Division 2 Factory Licence or a Manufacturing Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796704 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796705 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[competent person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[competent person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person described in
    subsection 122(2). ([personne compétente]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[division 2 factory licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[division 2 factory licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence
    that is issued by the Minister under paragraph 7(1)(a) of the
    [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17) authorizing a manufacturing
    activity referred to in section 53 at a workplace. ([licence de
    fabrique de la section 2]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[manufacturing certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[manufacturing certificate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    certificate that is issued by the Minister under paragraph 7(1)(c)
    of the [Explosives Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and authorizes an activity
    referred to in section 107 at a workplace. ([certificat de
    fabrication]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[worker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[worker]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is at a workplace
    to carry out a manufacturing operation or some other kind of work
    (for example, maintenance of facilities or repair of equipment) for
    the holder of a division 2 factory
    licence. ([travailleur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[workplace]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[workplace]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a building, room or area
    where an activity involving the manufacture of explosives, including
    their storage, is carried out. ([lieu de travail]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 11, 38
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400259){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400259.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106-20160415.html "Link to previous version of section 106"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION a]{.HLabel3}[Authorized Activities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796715 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized activities

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of a division 2 factory licence or manufacturing certificate
  may carry out those of the following activities that are specified in
  the licence or certificate at a workplace specified in the licence or
  certificate:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the owner of a surface mine or
    quarry, the blending of ammonium nitrate and fuel oil at a blast
    site at the mine or quarry;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of small arms cartridges for sale,
    and the storage of up to 225 kg of explosives contained in the
    cartridges and up to 75 kg of propellant powder in bulk to be used
    in manufacturing the cartridges;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of any explosives for the purpose
    of conducting an experiment, demonstration, test or analysis at a
    school, college, university or other learning institution or by a
    law enforcement or government agency and the storage of up to 5 kg
    of the manufactured explosives;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of any explosives for the purpose
    of conducting an experiment, test or analysis by a private or
    commercial laboratory and the storage of up to 5 kg of the
    manufactured explosives;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of consumer fireworks for personal
    use and the storage up to 25 kg of the manufactured fireworks;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of display fireworks for personal
    use and the storage up to 25 kg of the manufactured fireworks;

  - [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of F.5 novelty devices for
    personal use;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture of rocket motors for personal use
    and the storage of up to 25 kg of manufactured rocket motors and
    propellant powder (combined quantity) to be used in manufacturing
    the motors;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the manufacture and storage of black powder charges
    for ceremonial use;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the preparation and storage of display fireworks at
    a location other than the site of the display;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the mixing of non-explosive components for the
    purpose of manufacturing industrial explosives at the site where
    they will be used;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the preparation and packaging of assortments of
    explosives for the purpose of sale by a person who does not hold a
    vendor magazine licence; or

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} any other activity relating to the manufacture and
    storage of explosives (for example, assembling a net-throwing device
    for sale, re-packing deteriorated explosives or destroying
    explosives).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transportation of black powder charges

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of transporting black powder charges
  manufactured under paragraph (1)(h), the charges are classified as UN
  0027.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 14
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400263){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400263.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 27](#1451431-1452383){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451431-1452383.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-107-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 107"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder of a manufacturing certificate may acquire an explosive if it
  is specified in the certificate and will be used to manufacture
  another explosive whose manufacture is authorized by the certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a division 2 factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate that authorizes the storage of an explosive
  must comply with the terms and conditions of the licence or
  certificate. The holder must also comply with the provisions that
  apply to storage in Parts 10 to 18. However, they are not required to
  comply with those provisions if the explosive is stored at the
  workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a division 2 factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate that authorizes the manufacture of an
  explosive for sale may sell that explosive. The holder must comply
  with the terms and conditions of the licence or certificate and with
  the provisions that apply to sale in Parts 10 to 18.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A holder of a division 2 factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate that authorizes the manufacture of fireworks
  must comply with the provisions that apply to the use of fireworks in
  Parts 16 to 18.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400267){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400267.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION b]{.HLabel3}[Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796744 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for licence or certificate

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant for a division 2 factory licence or a manufacturing
  certificate must complete, sign and send to the Chief Inspector of
  Explosives the application form provided by the Department of Natural
  Resources. The application must state whether the application is for a
  licence or a certificate and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email
    address of both the applicant and a contact person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of explosive, and the quantity, to be
    manufactured or stored;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the application is for a certificate, the period
    for which the certificate is requested;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the workplace, all equipment in
    the workplace that is related to the manufacture of explosives and
    all protective barriers in the workplace;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of all equipment in the workplace
    that is not related to the manufacture of explosives, but could
    increase the likelihood of an ignition; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the number of people authorized in the workplace
    when explosives are present.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Site plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a site plan that shows

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of the workplace within any building
    or structure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the topography of any outdoor area included in the
    workplace;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the location of the barriers and equipment
    described in paragraphs (1)(d) and (e); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between the barriers and
    equipment described in paragraphs (1)(d) and (e).

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the location of the workplace within the locality;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the area surrounding the site that is exposed to
    the hazards (for example, debris or blast effect) that could result
    from an ignition of the explosives to be manufactured or stored at
    the site;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} each vulnerable place within that area; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between the workplace and
    each vulnerable place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The applicant for a division 2 factory licence or a
  manufacturing certificate must pay the applicable fees set out in Part
  19.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400272){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400272.html"
  role="button"}

  role="button"}

#### [SUBDIVISION c]{.HLabel3}[Requirements for Holders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796766 .Subheading}

##### [Workplace]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796767 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of holder

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate must ensure that the
requirements relating to the workplace set out in sections 111 to 115
are met in the workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means of escape

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  workplace must have a means of escape that will permit all people in
  the workplace to leave it quickly and easily in an emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lighting, electrical and heating
  systems

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The lighting, electrical and heating systems that are
  used in the workplace must not increase the likelihood of an ignition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compatibility

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every thing that is in a workplace
  must be made from materials that are compatible with the explosives
  and raw material in the workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incompatible things

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, a thing that is not compatible with an
  explosive or raw material in the workplace but is required for
  manufacturing may be brought to the workplace for immediate use. It
  must be removed as soon as the circumstances permit after its use,
  unless the division 2 factory licence or manufacturing certificate
  authorizes its storage in the workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Open-flame device

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An open-flame device or open-element electrical
  appliance must not be stored in a part of the workplace where
  explosives are being manufactured.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400276){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400276.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-112-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 112"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Activities prohibited

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  explosives are being manufactured or stored at a workplace, no other
  activities may be carried out there.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Competent person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All manufacturing operations at the workplace must be
  carried out by a competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition of workplace

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The workplace must be kept clean, dry and organized.
  Any spill of explosives, raw material or other material must be
  cleaned up as soon as the circumstances permit to eliminate any
  possibility of an ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign matter

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the likelihood of an ignition could be increased
  as a result of foreign matter (for example, bolts, gravel or grit)
  being present in or mixed with any raw material, or explosive used as
  raw material, the material must be carefully examined and passed
  through a sieve or treated to remove or exclude the foreign matter
  before manufacturing begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Containers

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} All raw material, explosive substances and explosives
  waste must be kept in closed containers that prevent spills and
  contamination. The contents of each container must be clearly
  identified on a label attached to the container.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of waste and contaminated
  material

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} All explosives waste and explosives-contaminated
  material must be destroyed in a manner that does not increase the
  likelihood of an accidental ignition during or after the destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of explosives

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Unless the division 2 factory licence or
  manufacturing certificate provides otherwise, explosives must be
  removed as soon as the circumstances permit after they are
  manufactured from the part of the workplace where they were
  manufactured to the part of the workplace where they will be stored.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decontamination

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A workplace that is no longer being used to
  manufacture explosives must be decontaminated as soon as the
  circumstances permit. Contaminated equipment at the workplace must be
  decontaminated before it is removed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Thunderstorms

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} On the approach of a thunderstorm, all manufacturing
  operations in the workplace must be shut down and all people in the
  workplace must be immediately moved to a safe place. Until the storm
  passes, the operations must remain shut down and the people must not
  be permitted to return.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400284){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400284.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-113-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 113"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Maintenance and repair work done at
  or to a workplace, or to equipment in it, must be carried out by a
  competent person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hazardous work

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any work that involves the use of a device that
  produces heat, flame or sparks or involves grinding or impact must be
  done in a manner that does not increase the likelihood of an ignition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warning sign

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** A sign that warns
against unauthorized entry must be posted at each entrance to the
workplace.

##### [Labelling]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796808 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of holder

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate must ensure that the
labelling requirements set out in section 117 are met at the workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information displayed on explosives

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following information must be legibly printed on each explosive that
  is manufactured in the workshop, or if that is not possible, on a
  label affixed to the explosive or, if even that is not possible, on
  the packaging containing the explosive or a label affixed to its
  packaging:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product name of the explosive and the name and
    address of the person who obtained its authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the explosive was manufactured
    and, if the manufacturer carries out manufacturing operations in
    shifts, the shift during which it was manufactured; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} instructions, in both English and French, for its
    safe handling, storage, use and destruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information on packaging

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following information must be legibly printed on
  the packaging containing the explosive or on a label affixed to its
  packaging:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words "Ammunition/[Munitions]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}", "Explosives/[Explosifs]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Fireworks/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    "Pyrotechnics/[Pièces pyrotechniques]{.otherLang lang="fr"}" or
    "Reloaded Cartridges/[Cartouches rechargées]{.otherLang lang="fr"}",
    or other descriptive wording, as the case may be, on the outer
    packaging and any inner packaging;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the product name of the explosive and the name and
    address of the person who obtained its authorization, on the outer
    packaging;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type F explosive, whether it is
    type F.1, F.2, F.3, F.4 or F.5, on the outer packaging; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a type S explosive, whether it is
    type S.1 or S.2, on the outer packaging.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 12, 39
- SOR/2018-231, ss. 43, 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400288){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400288.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 29](#1451435-1452388){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451435-1452388.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-117-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Safety of People at the Workplace]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796825 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of holder

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate must ensure that the
requirements relating to personal safety set out in sections 119 and 120
are met at the workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervision

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** Every person at a
workplace, other than a worker, must be kept under the direct
supervision of a competent person at all times.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal protective equipment

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person at a workplace must be
  required to wear the personal protective equipment, clothing and
  devices that are needed to protect them from the hazards to which they
  could be exposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hair, clothing and accessories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person at a workplace must be required to
  confine or cover any loose hair and to confine, cover or remove any
  loose clothing, jewellery or other accessories if the hair, clothing
  or accessories could increase the likelihood of an ignition or the
  likelihood of harm to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic devices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person at a workplace must be required to
  deactivate any electronic device in their possession (for example, a
  cellphone or two-way radio) in any part of the workplace where the
  device, if activated, could increase the likelihood of an ignition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person must not be authorized to enter the
  workplace if there are reasonable grounds to believe that they are
  under the influence of or are carrying alcohol or another
  performance-diminishing substance. However, a person who has taken a
  prescription drug may be authorized to enter if they have medical
  proof that the drug is needed and that it will not impede their
  ability to function safely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No smoking

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Smoking must be prohibited in the workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No fire-producing devices

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Fire-producing devices (for example, matches and
  lighters) must not be permitted in the workplace unless they are
  authorized by the division 2 factory licence or manufacturing
  certificate.

##### [Knowledge of the Workplace]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796843 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of certificate holder

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate must ensure that the
training requirements set out in sections 122 and 123 are met at the
workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not be permitted to manufacture explosives at a workplace
  unless they meet the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are a competent person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are at least 18 years old, participating in a
    training program and under the direct supervision of a competent
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Competent person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A competent person is a person who meets the
  following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are at least 18 years old; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder of the division 2 licence or
    manufacturing certificate believes on reasonable grounds that the
    person understands the hazards to which they could be exposed in the
    workplace and is competent to carry out their duties in manner that
    is safe and lawful and ensures the security of the workplace.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400292){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400292.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 30](#1451440-1452390){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451440-1452390.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** A training record and a
record of work experience must be kept for each worker for two years
after the date on which it is made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Knowledge

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or manufacturing certificate must have knowledge of
the following matters and must ensure that these matters are
communicated to the workers at the workplace:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and these Regulations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions of the licence or
  certificate, including the maximum quantity of explosives and raw
  material and the maximum number of people authorized to be in the
  workplace at any one time;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} all safety rules respecting the manufacture of
  explosives at the workplace;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the personal protective equipment that is needed to
  protect people from the hazards to which they could be exposed;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} all emergency plans for the workplace, including the
  evacuation plan;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the hazards of, and safe handling practices for, the
  explosives and raw material at the workplace;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the precautions that must be taken to minimize the
  likelihood of harm to people and property during manufacturing
  operations, including harm resulting from chemical incompatibility;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the operation of the equipment at the workplace;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance and repair of the equipment,
  including decontamination; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} procedures for inspecting the workplace or equipment
  after maintenance and repair work has been done.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400297){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400297.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Management of the Workplace]{.HTitleText4} {#h-796869 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities of holder

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** A holder of a division
2 factory licence or a manufacturing certificate must ensure that the
management requirements set out in sections 126 to 128 are met at the
workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating procedures

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** Operating procedures
must be put in place for each of the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} every activity specified in the division 2 factory
  licence or manufacturing certificate;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the destruction of explosives, explosives waste and
  explosives-contaminated material;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the management of spills and their clean-up;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the response to emergency situations;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the decontamination of the workplace and all tools
  and equipment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400302){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400302.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** A record of each
explosive in the workplace (including any explosive that is used as raw
material) must be kept for two years after the date on which the record
is made. It must set out, for each explosive,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the product name, UN number and a short description
  of the explosive;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was acquired, the quantity acquired
  and the date of acquisition;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was used to manufacture an
  explosive, the quantity used and the date of use;

- [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} if the manufactured explosive referred to in
  paragraph (c) was used at the workplace, the quantity used and the
  date of use;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was manufactured in the workplace,
  the quantity manufactured and the date of manufacture;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was stored, the quantity stored, the
  place in which it was stored and the dates on which it was placed in
  and removed from storage;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was shipped from the workplace, the
  quantity shipped, the date of shipment and the address of the person
  to whom it was shipped; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive was destroyed at the workplace, the
  quantity destroyed and the date of destruction.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 39
- SOR/2018-231, s. 44(F)
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400306){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400306.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 31](#1451442-1452394){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451442-1452394.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- licence or certificate

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  copy of the division 2 factory licence or the manufacturing
  certificate and all documents that are referred to in the licence or
  certificate must be kept at the workplace.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- Division 2

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of this Division must be made available to
  workers at the workplace.

#### [SUBDIVISION d]{.HLabel3}[Requirements for People at a Workplace]{.HTitleText3} {#h-796895 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Visitors

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before entering, a visitor to a
  workplace must obtain the permission of the holder of the division 2
  factory licence or manufacturing certificate. They must remain under
  the supervision of a competent person at all times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Performance-diminishing substance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not enter a workplace if they are under
  the influence of or are carrying alcohol or another
  performance-diminishing substance. However, a person who has taken a
  prescription drug may enter if they have medical proof that they need
  the drug and that it will not impede their ability to function safely.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal protective equipment

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person at a workplace must
  wear the personal protective equipment, clothing and devices that are
  needed to protect them from the hazards to which they could be
  exposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hair, clothing and accessories

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person at a workplace must confine or cover any
  loose hair and confine, cover or remove any loose clothing, jewellery
  or other accessories if the hair, clothing or accessories could
  increase the likelihood of an ignition or the likelihood of harm to
  the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic devices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person must deactivate any electronic device in
  their possession (for example, a cellphone or two-way radio) in any
  part of the workplace where the device, if activated, could increase
  the likelihood of an ignition.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No smoking

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** A person must not smoke
at a workplace.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not manufacture explosives at a workplace unless they meet
  the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are a competent person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are at least 18 years old, participating in a
    training program and under the direct supervision of a competent
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other tasks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not carry out a task at a workplace
  unless they have been trained in that task and understand the hazards
  to which they could be exposed. They must take the precautions that
  they are directed to take to minimize the likelihood of an accidental
  ignition.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400310){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400310.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 32](#1451447-1452396){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451447-1452396.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-132-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 132"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Manufacturing that Does Not Require a Licence or Certificate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-796917 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** A person who
manufactures an explosive under the authority of this Division must
comply with Parts 10 to 18.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400314){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400314.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-133-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 133"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Experiments

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  school, college, university or other learning institution, or a law
  enforcement or government agency, that complies with subsection (2)
  may manufacture up to 50 g of explosives for the purposes of an
  experiment, demonstration, test or analysis.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The institution or agency must ensure that the
  following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manufacturing must be carried out with the
    consent of the management of the institution or agency by one of its
    employees or by a person who is at all times under the direct
    supervision of an employee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee must know how to carry out the
    manufacturing in a manner that minimizes the likelihood of harm to
    people and property and be aware of the precautions that must be
    taken to minimize the likelihood of harm to people and property;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} every person who carries out the manufacturing must
    be at least 18 years old;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} precautions that will eliminate any possibility of
    an accidental ignition must be taken;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} explosives must not be removed from the location
    where they are manufactured except for the purpose of destroying
    them; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} all explosives must be destroyed in a manner that
    does not increase the likelihood of an accidental ignition during or
    after the destruction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400318){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400318.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-134-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 134"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assembling explosives for use

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who complies with subsection (2) may assemble explosives by
  combining explosive articles (for example, a detonator with a booster,
  a detonating cord with an explosive cartridge or fireworks with
  fireworks accessories) at the place where the explosives will be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosive articles must be on the list of
    authorized explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the explosive articles must not be altered, except
    that cartridges may be cut or slit and detonating cord and fuses may
    be cut or trimmed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    accidental ignition must be taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400322){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400322.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-135-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 135"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Christmas crackers

- **[[135.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who complies with
  subsection (2) may assemble Christmas crackers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the snap to be used in the cracker must be on the
    list of authorized explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount of explosive in each cracker must be
    less than 2 mg; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    accidental ignition must be taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2018-231, s. 15
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400325){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400325.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-135.1-20181102.html "Link to previous version of section 135.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Underground activities

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who complies with subsection (2) may carry out any of the
  following activities underground at an underground mine or underground
  construction project:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pneumatically transferring explosives;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pumping, thickening or gassing either emulsion
    explosives or water gel explosives while charging boreholes; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} blending emulsion explosives or water gel
    explosives with ammonium nitrate or ammonium nitrate/fuel oil
    mixtures while charging boreholes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosives must be on the list of authorized
    explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the equipment used to pump, thicken, gas or blend
    emulsion explosives or water gel explosives must be designed to
    minimize the likelihood of an ignition, including an ignition
    resulting from pumping against a blocked outlet or from running the
    pump without any feed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all progressive cavity pumps must be equipped with
    at least two independent safety shutdown systems to prevent an
    excessive rise in temperature;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the person is assisted by another person, the
    other person must be trained to operate the equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a preventive maintenance procedure must be put in
    place for the equipment, including the pumps;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} maintenance must be performed by workers who are
    knowledgeable about the equipment to be maintained; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    ignition must be taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400329){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400329.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-136-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 136"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pneumatic transfer of explosives

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who complies with subsection (2) may pneumatically transfer
  explosives at a surface mine or a quarry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosives must be on the list of authorized
    explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the device used for pneumatic charging must have a
    maximum capacity of 100 kg and the explosives used in the pneumatic
    charging must be in bags, each with a maximum capacity of 30 kg; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    ignition must be taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400333){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400333.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multi-ingredient kits

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may mix together the ingredients of a multi-ingredient kit if
  they comply with subsection (2) and if the kit is not a reactive
  target.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the kit must be on the list of authorized
    explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the mixing must be carried out at the place where
    the explosive to be manufactured will be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    ignition must be taken;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive to be manufactured is classified
    as type F.3, the person mixing the ingredients must hold a fireworks
    operator certificate --- pyrotechnician; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the explosive to be manufactured is a special
    purpose explosive, the person mixing the ingredients must hold a
    fireworks operator certificate --- pyrotechnician or a Firearms
    Possession and Acquisition Licence issued under the [Firearms
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11.6).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400339){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400339.html"
  role="button"}

  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 145](#1451822-1452757){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451822-1452757.html"

:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reactive targets

- **[[138.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person may mix together the
  ingredients of a reactive target kit if they comply with subsection
  (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reactive target kit must be on the list of
    authorized explosives referred to in subsection 41(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the mixing must be carried out at the place where
    the explosive to be manufactured will be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    ignition must be taken;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person mixing the ingredients must hold a
    Firearms Possession and Acquisition Licence issued under the
    [Firearms Act](/eng/acts/F-11.6);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the instructions displayed on the packaging for
    mixing the ingredients must be followed and if there are no such
    instructions, the ingredients in the reactive target kit must not be
    mixed or used;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} foreign matter must not be added to the reactive
    target kit when mixing the ingredients or before use;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} only one reactive target kit must be mixed at a
    time;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a reactive target kit may not be combined with
    other reactive target kits or foreign matter; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} once mixed, a reactive target kit must not be
    stored or transported.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 34](#1451454-1452415){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451454-1452415.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Spills or accidents

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who complies with subsection (2) may remove explosives from, or
  repackage explosives at, the site of an accident or spill.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} precautions that minimize the likelihood of an
    ignition must be taken;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any packaging used must prevent the explosives
    involved from leaking or spilling and minimize the likelihood of an
    accidental ignition; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Inspector of Explosives must be notified
    of the accident or spill within 12 hours after the removal or
    repackaging begins.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400343){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400343.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency response assistance plan

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may pump explosives numbered UN 0332, as classified by the
  Chief Inspector of Explosives under the [Explosives
  Act](/eng/acts/E-17), and explosives numbered UN 3375 in activating an
  emergency response assistance plan approved by the Minister of
  Transport under the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act,
  1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01) if they comply with subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} written permission must be obtained from a holder
    of a division 1 factory licence that authorizes the manufacture of
    blasting explosives in bulk to store the pumped explosives at the
    holder's factory and to decontaminate the pumping equipment there;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pump that is safe for pumping the explosives must
    be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the explosives and contaminated equipment must be
    stored at the factory; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the follow-up report that is required
    under Part 8 of the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2001-286) must be submitted to the
    Chief Inspector of Explosives within 30 days after the date on which
    the explosives are pumped.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400348){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400348.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 35](#1451456-1452419){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451456-1452419.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-140-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 140"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Industrial explosives

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who complies with subsection (2) may destroy deteriorated,
  expired or misfired industrial explosives by placing them in a
  borehole with other explosives and igniting the other explosives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who carries out the activity must ensure
  that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the explosives to be destroyed and the other
    explosives must have similar properties (for example, similar
    density and propensity to detonate); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the presence of the other explosives at the time of
    the ignition must not increase the likelihood of harm to people or
    property.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400352){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400352.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 36(F)](#1451463-1452420){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451463-1452420.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20230603.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction

**[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** A government or law
enforcement agency (for example, a police explosives disposal unit) may
break up, unmake or destroy an explosive if it does so in the course of
its duties.

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[Magazine Licences and Storage in a Licensed Magazine]{.HTitleText1} {#h-797008 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overview

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out how
to obtain a vendor magazine licence, a user magazine licence or a user
magazine zone licence. It also sets out the rules applicable to holders
of these licences.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[distribution establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[distribution establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    establishment where explosives are stored for sale to distributors
    or retailers, whether or not the explosives are sold to
    users. ([établissement de distribution]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[magazine site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[magazine site]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the area, including any
    building or structure, that is used in connection with the storage
    of explosives in a magazine. ([site de poudrière]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[retail establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[retail establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an establishment
    where explosives are stored for sale that is not a distribution
    establishment. ([établissement de vente au détail]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[user magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[user magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence that
    is issued under paragraph 7(1)(a) of the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and authorizes the storage of explosives by a
    person who has acquired them for use or conveyance. ([licence de
    poudriére (utilisateur)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[user magazine zone licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[user magazine zone licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence
    that is issued under paragraph 7(1)(a) of the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and authorizes the storage of type E or I
    explosives by a person who has acquired them for use. It also
    authorizes the holder to move the storage of explosives from one
    site to another. ([licence de poudrière
    (utilisateur-zone)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[vendor magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vendor magazine licence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a licence that
    is issued under paragraph 7(1)(a) of the [Explosives
    Act](/eng/acts/E-17) and that authorizes the storage of explosives
    by a person who has acquired them for sale or for sale and
    use. ([licence de poudrière (vendeur)]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-144-20140201.html "Link to previous version of section 144"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-797021 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for magazine licence

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant for a magazine licence must complete, sign and send to the
  Chief Inspector of Explosives the application form provided by the
  Department of Natural Resources. The application must state whether a
  vendor magazine licence, a user magazine licence or a user magazine
  zone licence is requested and must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name, address, telephone number and email
    address of both the applicant and a contact person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the address and geographical coordinates of the
    magazine site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the number of magazines for which the licence is
    requested;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of each type of explosive to be stored
    in each magazine;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the application is for a vendor magazine
    licence, an indication of whether the site will be a retail
    establishment or a distribution establishment; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the site will be a distribution establishment,
    an indication of whether explosives will be repackaged there.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Site plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a plan of the magazine site that shows

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the location of each magazine and each vulnerable
      place at the site as well as the location of each vulnerable place
      outside the site that is exposed to the hazards (for example,
      debris or blast effect) that could result from ignition of the
      explosives to be stored at the site, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between each magazine at
      the site, between each magazine and each vulnerable place at the
      site as well as between each magazine at the site and each
      vulnerable place outside the site that is exposed to the hazards
      (for example, debris or blast effect) that could result from
      ignition of the explosives to be stored at the site; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more magazines occupies only a part of a
    building or structure, a drawing that shows the location of each
    magazine in the building or structure and the location of each
    entrance to and exit from the room or area in which each magazine is
    located and to which the public has access.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Site description

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application must include the following
  information about the site:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the proposed use of the site and
    the proposed use of each building and structure at the site;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between each magazine and
    any potential source of ignition at the site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the distance in metres between each magazine at the
    site, between each magazine and each vulnerable place at the site as
    well as between each magazine at the site and each vulnerable place
    outside the site that is exposed to the hazards (for example, debris
    or blast effect) that could result from ignition of the explosives
    to be stored at the site;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the minimum distance in metres that must be
    maintained between each magazine at the site and each vulnerable
    place shown on the site plan, as set out in the National Standard of
    Canada Standard CAN/BNQ 2910--510/2015 entitled Explosives ---
    Quantity Distances as amended from time to time;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the safety and security features
    of the site (for example, signs, alarm systems, barriers, fencing
    and berms); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for each magazine at the site,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the tag number, if any, issued by the Explosives
      Regulatory Division, Department of Natural Resources,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the applicable magazine type number, as set out
      in the National Standard of Canada Standard CAN/BNQ 2910--500/2015
      entitled Explosives --- Magazines for Industrial Explosives, as
      amended from time to time or, if the magazine does not correspond
      to any of those types, its specifications, including its
      construction materials and its safety and security features, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} its internal dimensions (length, width and
      height) in metres to the nearest 0.1 m.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire safety plan

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a fire safety plan that
  sets out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measures to be taken to minimize the likelihood
    of a fire at the site and to control the spread of any fire;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the emergency procedures to be followed in case of
    a fire, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} activation of the alarms,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} notification of the fire department, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} evacuation procedures, including evacuation
      routes and safe assembly places;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a fire should or should
    not be fought and a procedure for determining whether a fire should
    be fought; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the measures to be taken to train employees in the
    measures, procedures and circumstances described in the plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Site security plan

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If type E, I or D explosives are to be stored at the
  site, the application must include a security plan that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an assessment of the security risks resulting from
    the presence of explosives at the site;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the measures to be taken to
    minimize those risks;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the procedures to be followed in
    response to security incidents; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the procedures to be followed to
    report security incidents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marine flare destruction plan

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the site is a distribution establishment at which
  marine flares (type S.1 or S.2) are to be stored, the application must
  include a plan for destroying expired marine flares that are returned
  to the site. The plan must set out where and how the marine flares
  will be stored and destroyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identifier

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every magazine and vulnerable place that is shown on
  a site plan must be identified by a number, letter or distinctive
  name, which must be used to identify the magazine or vulnerable place
  on the site plan and in the site description. The identifier of the
  magazine must be posted on the exterior of the magazine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scale drawing

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Every drawing or plan must be drawn to scale or be a
  reasonable approximation of actual distances and dimensions and must
  include a legend.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial site

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the application is for a user magazine zone
  licence, the requirements of subsections (1) to (8) apply to the
  initial magazine site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An applicant for a magazine licence must pay the
  applicable fees set out in Part 19.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2016-75, ss. 40, 41
- [SOR/2022-121, s. 4](#1354349-1400360){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1354349-1400360.html"
  role="button"}
- [SOR/2024-77, s. 37](#1451465-1452424){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1451465-1452424.html"
  role="button"}
:::
